Got it. Here's the inbox slice with email removed:

**Inbox and reminders (FR16, FR01), in-app only**

- The `notifications` table already exists (slice 3) and already gets limit and request notices. New kinds: `allowance_due` and `handover_reminder`.
- **Reminders.** The hourly pg_cron job that creates due allowances also sends the notices. A `due` notice goes out on the due day, then a `reminder` every 3 days while the allowance is still unpaid (D4). Each one is sent once only, keyed by occurrence and date. Parents get both notices. My guess is the child gets the due notice too ("Your allowance is due"), but not the reminders, so the child never feels like they're chasing their parent.
- **Inbox screen for both roles,** with an unread badge in the shell. For parents it covers all their children (D12). Opening a notice shows the record as it is now, not as it was when the notice was sent. A reminder for an allowance that has since been paid shows "Handed over".
- **Mark read** when a notice is opened, plus "mark all read".
- **Dropped:** Resend email, `notification_deliveries`, the dispatcher route and the `/api/cron` fallback. pg_cron covers all of it. Email moves to the final demo alongside web push.

FR16 was already written as in-app first, so dropping email doesn't put any acceptance criterion at risk. I'll update the roadmap and the first-milestone spec to match.

What's wrong or missing there? And moving on to the savings goal: when a kid opens Jarso and looks at their goal, what should they see?

Here's the goal screen as I understand it:

**Savings goal (FR02, FR03, FR12)**

- **One active goal per child** (domain rule). The child names it ("New football boots") and enters the price.
- **The card shows:** name, target, a progress bar, amount saved, amount left, and "about N more allowances".
- **Saved** is the Save balance minus reserved withdrawals. Money that's due but not handed over doesn't count, and neither do pending deposits (FR02).
- **Payments left** comes from the child's plan: "I'll save £X from each allowance". The estimate is `ceil(left / X)`, and it updates whenever the plan or the balance changes. My guess: with no plan yet, it says "Set a plan to see how long it'll take" rather than guessing.
- **Reaching the goal.** My guess is one calm celebration on the child's screen (no confetti, per the gamification rules), then a choice: keep the goal or set a new one. Nothing gets spent automatically, because buying the item is just a withdrawal request.
- **Also on the goal:** a "Your goal" next step on child Home, and a pending withdrawal shown as "£X reserved, not counted yet".
- **Not in this slice:** what-if previews before deciding. That's FR05, in the final demo.

What's wrong or missing? One question decides how this connects to the money. The child's "save £X per allowance" plan: should it prefill the Save amount on the parent's handover screen, or stay as the child's own estimate that the parent can see but that doesn't change the handover?

The research agent has reported back. It didn't verify everything it set out to check (notes at the end). Here are the main findings and what they change:

**What the evidence says**
- **Put one next step first.** Duolingo rebuilt its home screen around a single guided path because learners didn't know which step to take next ([blog](https://blog.duolingo.com/new-duolingo-home-screen-design/)). This supports the "Do this next" card.
- **Show balances right under it.** Every kids' money app checked (GoHenry, Greenlight, Rooster Money, Revolut <18) is built around the balance.
- **Make the goal the child's own.** Rooster Money lets a child add a photo and a colour to a goal ([source](https://roostermoney.com/savings-pots-insights-2025/)). No study favours a ring, a bar or a road for this age group, so the plain bar is fine.
- **Streaks hurt kids.**
  - A 2026 study of children aged 6–12 using Duolingo found their goal turned into "keeping the streak", with "streak panic" at bedtime ([IDC 2026](https://dl.acm.org/doi/full/10.1145/3773077.3806127)).
  - The UK ICO children's code says children should be able to take breaks "without losing their progress" ([Std 5](https://ico.org.uk/for-organisations/uk-gdpr-guidance-and-resources/childrens-information/childrens-code-guidance-and-resources/age-appropriate-design-a-code-of-practice-for-online-services/5-detrimental-use-of-data/)).
- **Avoid:**
  - losing lives on a wrong answer (Greenlight);
  - points that turn into gift cards (Zogo);
  - goal confetti (Greenlight);
  - "don't miss out" wording.

**Changes I'd make to the mockup**
1. **Drop the Mon–Sun day blocks.** Grey empty days still read like a streak. Instead show progress that only goes up: "3 skills learned · 2 missions this week", with skill chips that stay earned for good.
2. **Goal gets an emoji or photo** the child picks, next to the name.
3. Everything else stays as it is: next step first, money second, plain bar with "£19 to go, about 5 more allowances".

**Not verified:** what sits at the top of each money app's home screen, and the apps BusyKid, Step, Acorns Early, Till, Monzo teen, Osper, Khan Academy Kids and Prodigy.

Should I update the web view with those two changes, then do the parent Home the same way?

The reviewer's report is back. Most of it I've folded into the doc myself. These are the calls I made, for you to check when you read it:

- **Gifts send no notices.** The child must never see a pending gift, so gifts stay out of the child's inbox and out of the "on its way" coin.
- **Reminders continue for any allowance still due,** even if the schedule was paused afterwards, because the money is still owed. Nothing is backfilled.
- **A pending request shows as a chip under its jar,** not in "Do this next". Examples: "£5 asked from Save · waiting for your parent", and "£3 to Save · give it to your parent".
- **The jar fill floor is a fixed 20 units of the family's currency.** No exchange rates are involved.
- **The goal plan is worded "each time",** never "a week", because allowances can be monthly or yearly.
- **The weekly learning-days line on the Learn page stays as it is.** It turned out to live there, not on Home, and it has no loss message.
- **The roadmap and `design.md` get updated:** no Figma step, no milestone road and no ring (the jar's goal line replaces them), and no Jars or Requests tabs.

One question is yours. Today's child Home has four things the jar design doesn't place: the "Ask the money guide" button, purchases waiting to sync offline, purchase history with Undo, and the allowance card (schedule and pause message). My suggestion:
- the guide button moves to the header;
- the offline banner sits above the jars;
- the allowance schedule and any pause become one line under the shelf ("£5 every Friday");
- history with Undo moves to a new **Diary** tab.

The Diary tab is new work, though it's where the final demo's diary screen will grow anyway. Should history move to a Diary tab now, or stay as a list below the jars on Home for Demo 2?

The design is ready for you to approve: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-30-inbox-goal-jar-home-design.md`. The mockup it points to is `docs/superpowers/specs/mockups/2026-10-30-jar-home.html`.

It now says history and Undo move to a new Diary tab at `/child/diary`, reusing the existing list and Undo button. That page is where the final demo's diary will grow.

**In short**
- **Inbox:** in-app only, for both roles, with an unread badge. Parents get a due notice, then a reminder every 3 days. The child gets only the due notice. Opening a notice shows the record as it is now. Email is dropped until the final demo.
- **Goal:** one per child, with a name, emoji, price and the child's own plan. Saved = Save balance minus money waiting to come out. Payments left = what's left divided by the plan, rounded up. The parent can see it but not edit it.
- **Jar Home:**
  - "Do this next", then Spend and Save jars, then a skills strip.
  - The Save jar fills up to the goal line.
  - Due money floats above the jars as a coin.
  - Parent Home leads with "Needs you", then each child's mini jars.

**Calls I made, worth checking while you read**
- **Who gets reminders:** the child gets the due notice only, never the reminders.
- **Goal ownership:** only the child can create or edit their goal.
- **Reaching the goal:** one quiet moment on the child's Home, then a choice between using the savings or setting a new goal. Nothing happens automatically.
- **"Do this next" order:** goal reached, then an approved withdrawal, then the existing learning order, then "Set a goal".
- **Spend jar fill:** both jars share one scale.
- **Plus the six calls from the reviewer round:** gifts, reminder states, pending-request chips, the 20-unit floor, "each time" wording, and the Learn page.

**Order of work:** slice 1 (deposits and withdrawals) goes first. Inbox and goal can then run in parallel, and the jar Home goes last.

Nothing is committed yet. Once you approve the design, I'll write the implementation plan. Anything to change first?

I've written the implementation plan and checked it against the design. It's saved at `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-30-inbox-goal-jar-home.md`: 13 tasks in three PRs.

- **PR C, inbox (Tasks 1–4):**
  - new notice kinds and `send_handover_reminders` on the hourly cron, with mark-as-read in SQL;
  - the inbox server code and notice links;
  - inbox pages for both roles with the unread badge;
  - docs updated to say no email.
- **PR D, goal (Tasks 5–7):**
  - the `goals` table, its write functions, and one pure function for progress and payments left;
  - the goals feature module;
  - the goal sheet with a live "about N more allowances" preview, and the plan note on the parent's handover screen.
- **PR E, jar Home (Tasks 8–13):**
  - `JarIllustration`;
  - the jar scale and the order of "Do this next";
  - the child Home rebuilt around the jars, and the Diary tab with history and Undo;
  - the parent Home with "Needs you" and mini jars;
  - updates to `design.md`, the roadmap and the FR audit, and a final full verify.

**Dependency:** PR D and PR E need slice 4 (deposits and withdrawals, from the existing plan) merged first, because the goal subtracts money waiting to come out of Save. PR C can start now.

**Five risks are each pinned to a test:**
- a parent hands over between reminder runs;
- two parents, one reads a notice;
- the goal price is edited below what's already saved;
- a currency without pence, like JPY;
- a child with no allowance and no goal.

Nothing is committed yet. The design, mockup and plan files are still uncommitted, along with the session log.

There are two ways to run it:
- **Subagent-driven:** a fresh agent builds each task and a fresh reviewer checks it before the next one starts.
- **Native:** I build every task here, then one reviewer checks the whole branch at the end.

I recommend subagent-driven. Thirteen tasks pass names and types between them across SQL, server and UI, and a money or RLS mistake is expensive to ship, so a check after each task is worth the cost.

Does the plan look right, and which approach should we use? Should slice 4 be built first under its existing plan?

PR C (the in-app inbox and handover reminders) is built and passes its final whole-branch review with no open findings. Every task was reviewed and fixed until its reviewer signed off. It's on branch `feat/inbox-reminders` (19 commits, not pushed), and `pnpm check` passes (776 unit tests). The fix wave also ran the database tests, the build and the inbox end-to-end tests, all green.

**What it does**
- **Reminders:** an hourly job sends the parent a notice on the day an allowance is due, then a reminder every 3 days until it's handed over. The child gets only the first "your allowance is due" notice. Gifts never send a notice, so the child can't learn about one early.
- **Inbox:** a page for each role, newest first, with "Mark all as read" and "Show more". Opening a notice marks it read and takes you to the record as it is now. It still works offline: the notice opens even if marking it read fails.
- **Unread badge:** shown on the Inbox tab. It updates when you come back to the tab or open the inbox, and the tab bar no longer waits for the count before appearing.
- **Docs:** updated to say no email until the final demo. Nine of the 13 first-milestone criteria are now met and two are partly met (it was 7 met and 3 partly met).

**Calls I made without you, and what each costs if wrong**
1. I built PR C first. The goal (PR D) and the jar Home (PR E) wait for slice 4 (deposits and withdrawals), because the goal subtracts money waiting to come out of Save. If wrong, it's only a delay.
2. Notices about purchases point the child to Home until the Diary tab exists. Children get none of these today, so it's a one-line change later.
3. I kept the working files in `/tmp` and worked on a plain branch, because the helper scripts in `~/.claude` were blocked and this repo keeps Claude config inside the repo. No cost.
4. I worked on a branch in your main checkout, not a separate worktree, so it could share the local Supabase and `node_modules`. No cost while nothing else runs in parallel.
5. The FR audit describes the branch as if it were on `main`. That becomes true when the PR merges, as with earlier PRs; until then it overstates.
6. I fixed the badge lag with a refresh when you return to the tab or open the inbox, instead of rewording the design. The cost is one extra page fetch each time you return to the tab.
7. The empty-inbox illustration waits for PR E. An empty inbox looks plain until then.
8. I kept my task log in `/tmp`, because PR D and PR E still need it. No cost.

**Left as small follow-ups:**
- the refresh also fires on pages without a badge (settings, invite);
- nothing tests what happens when one reminder fails mid-run;
- a failed reminder run is logged but doesn't alert anyone.

How do you want to finish?

1. Merge back to `main` locally
2. Push and create a Pull Request
3. Keep the branch as-is (I'll handle it later)

Slice 4 (savings withdrawals and deposits) is built on branch `feat/savings-requests`: 39 commits, not pushed. All three final reviews came back with no critical findings, and their fixes passed a re-review. Every suite passes on the final tree:
- `pnpm check`: 851 unit tests;
- database tests: 942;
- full end-to-end run: 86 tests, including the new race tests;
- build: passed after the last code fixes.

**What it does**
- **Child:** "Take out" asks to move money out of Save, showing how much is available. "Put in" moves cash from Spend into Save. A requests page shows status chips and lets the child cancel a request still waiting.
- **Parent:** for each request they can approve, say not now, confirm the hand-over or "Didn't hand it over", and confirm a deposit or "Didn't get it". The rules page now has caps, auto-approve and auto-confirm, each per time, per week or per month.
- **Money safety:**
  - Money moves only on hand-over or confirmation, and posts exactly once.
  - Pending and approved withdrawals reserve Save, and pending deposits hold Spend.
  - Two parents acting at once can't overdraw. The race tests show five hand-overs at once post exactly once, and five different requests at once never overspend Save or the cap.
- **Also:**
  - Inbox notices go to the parent for new requests and to the child for decisions.
  - Moves between jars show in history as "Save → Spend", not as income.
  - The rules form is now readable by screen readers.
  - Fixed: the parent's Home errored while the profile dialog was open.

**Calls I made without you, and what each costs if wrong**
1. Request notices carry the amount, plus yes or no for decisions, so the inbox shows the right wording. If wrong: generic wording.
2. All request buttons use the offline-safe helper. No cost.
3. Child Home changes kept small, because PR E rebuilds it. If wrong: a little rework either way.
4. The plan's three reviewer agents became the final review: one whole-branch review plus the security and database reviewers. No cost.
5. No push or PR without your go-ahead. Costs one question.
6. Working files in `/tmp` and a branch in your main checkout, as for PR C. No cost.
7. Dropped the invented rules-form headings. Kept the extra toasts and sheet titles in the app's voice. If wrong: some copy edits.
8. When a request changed under the parent, they see "This request changed. Refresh to see where it is." and the list refreshes. If wrong: they don't get a more specific "not enough in Save" message.
9. The duplicate control names in the rules form were fixed now rather than deferred. No cost.
10. One race scenario in the plan can't happen, so I replaced it with a test that drops Save after approval. No cost.
11. A hand-over must leave the other approved withdrawals covered. If Save ever drops below all of them, nothing can be handed over until the parent cancels one. Normal use can't reach that state, but it must be revisited before any feature that can lower Save.
12. Cancelling doesn't record whether the child or the parent did it. If wrong: the data can't tell them apart.
13. Showing the parent a "reserved" total on the jars waits for PR E. No cost.

How do you want to finish?

1. Merge back to `main` locally
2. Push and create a Pull Request
3. Keep the branch as-is (I'll handle it later)

PR D (savings goal) is built on branch `feat/savings-goal`: 6 commits, not pushed. All checks pass on the final tree:
- `pnpm check`: 933 unit tests;
- build: passed;
- database tests: 982;
- full end-to-end run: 87.

**What it does**
- One goal per child, with a name, an emoji from a set of 24, a price and the child's own plan ("How much will you save each time?").
- **Progress** counts Save minus money waiting to come out, never due or pending money. "About N more allowances" updates as the plan or balance changes. The first time the goal is reached is recorded.
- **Goal sheet:** a live estimate as the child types, plus "Start a new goal" behind a confirm step.
- **Goal card on child Home:** a gold bar, "£26 saved. £19 to go.", and "Goal reached" when done.
- **Parent's handover screen:** shows "Maya plans to save £4 each time toward ⚽ Football boots". The starting split is unchanged.
- **Only the child can write the goal.** Parents can read it.

**Final review:** ready to merge, with no critical or important issues as the reviewer labelled them. I re-graded two of its minors by what a child would see and fixed them test-first:
- the plan hint showed "Up to 5.00" with no currency symbol;
- the bar could show full while money was still to go.

I also moved the "See your Save requests" link back with the request buttons, and marked FR02 as built in the audit.

**Calls I made, and what each costs if wrong**
1. A paused allowance still caps the plan; only a stopped one doesn't. If wrong: a paused child's plan is limited by the old allowance.
2. Without an allowance, a plan larger than the price is refused with its own message. If wrong: one more message.
3. The goal's progress figure comes from the database, which already subtracts reserved money, so the maths lives in one place. No cost.
4. The "too much" plan errors become messages under the plan field in the data layer, not in the action. No cost.
5. Added a goal card to child Home, which the plan didn't include, so the goal can be reached in this PR. PR E redraws it as the Save jar's goal line. If wrong: small rework in PR E.
6. The handover screen reads the goal best-effort, so a failed read never blocks a handover. No cost.
7. The price and plan fields got their own ids, because two amount fields on one form collided. No cost.
8. Re-graded the two minors above to important. No cost.
9. Moved the goal card below the requests link. That fix has no test of its own; the build passed. No cost.
10. Marked FR02 as built now, not in PR E, so the audit is true if this PR ships alone. No cost.

**Deferred minors**
- The emoji error isn't announced to screen readers.
- Two first-time saves at the same moment would show one a generic error.
- Test gaps: the "too much" plan message mapping, the over-the-price message, a sibling reading another child's progress, the new goal being active after archiving, and the "already saved enough" preview line.
- The handover page logs the raw error, while child Home logs only its name.
- Child Home looks up the available balances twice.

I didn't add the Jarso follow-up (its balance tools subtracting reserved money). You hadn't confirmed it, so it's still open.

I fixed CodeRabbit's one comment on PR #41 and pushed it to the PR branch as 41a281d.

**The issue:** saving a goal first tried an update and then an insert. If a child saved a goal for the first time from two tabs at once, or retried after a timeout, one save failed with the generic "We couldn't save your goal."

**The fix:** `save_goal` now does a single atomic insert-or-update, so the database itself guarantees one goal per child even when two saves land together. I edited the migration in place, which is safe because it isn't on production yet.

**Tests:**
- The goal database tests now include the missing check that a new goal is active after archiving the old one.
- The full database suite passes, and the unit tests pass (933).
- There's no automated test that fires two saves at once; the existing tests confirm saving twice and archiving still work.

CI is running on the push. I didn't post on GitHub.

Research prior art for the home dashboard of a pocket-money learning app for children aged 11–14 (Jarso: Spend and Save jars, one savings goal with progress and "payments left", a daily learning mission, skill badges, an inbox). We are choosing a layout for the child's Home screen and want evidence, not opinions.

Look at (web search + fetch product pages, app store screenshots descriptions, design case studies, Dribbble/Behance only if from real products):
1. Kid/teen money apps: GoHenry, Greenlight, Revolut <18, Rooster Money, BusyKid, Step, Acorns Early, Till, NatWest Rooster, Monzo teen, Osper. What leads their child home screen (balance? goal? tasks/chores? learning?) How do they show savings goals (bar, ring, jar, milestones)?
2. Gamified learning dashboards for this age: Duolingo (path, streaks, and the criticism of streak pressure), Khan Academy Kids/Khanmigo, Prodigy, Zogo, Greenlight Level Up. What leads: the next lesson or progress?
3. Child-safety / ethical design guidance relevant to gamification for under-16s: UK ICO Age Appropriate Design Code (nudge techniques, streaks), 5Rights, any research on loss-framed streaks harming kids, design guidance on rewards for understanding vs outcomes.
4. Any UX research on goal visualization for children (ring vs bar vs journey/milestone road) and on "next step first" home screens.

Our hard rules already decided: no losing streaks or "you missed a day", no countdowns, no random rewards, no leaderboards/comparisons, rewards for understanding and thoughtful decisions, never for amount of money; celebration once per moment, no confetti.

Report back (under 600 words): for each area, 2–4 concrete findings with source URLs; then a short "implications for Jarso child Home" list covering: what should lead the screen, how to show the goal, how to show learning progress without streak pressure, and anything we should avoid. Flag anything you could not verify. Do not write files.


[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=SUBAGENT_RESPONSE num=1 session=14c1a4e8-25b8-4869-859a-f799b43c74df agent=a7e283b8992f57bd3]
timestamp: 2026-10-30T15:49:39.809Z
time: 2026-10-30 15:49
chars: 6023

## 1. Kid and teen money apps
- **GoHenry** (App Store listing): the child app is built around balance, savings goals that parents can lock, task lists and "Money Missions" (videos, quizzes, points, badges, levels). It claims kids move "over 30% more into their savings in the first month after completing one" (vendor claim, unaudited). Goals have a target date and an automatic weekly transfer. https://apps.apple.com/us/app/gohenry-by-acorns-kids-banking/id683249974 · https://www.gohenry.com/uk/blog/financial-education/money-saving-habits-gohenry-money-missions
- **NatWest Rooster Money**: goals are "pots" the child personalises with a goal amount, a colour and their own photo. The child can flip a pot between the photo and the goal amount "for motivation". Their dashboard post says a Goal pot that shows goals on the child dashboard is still "being worked on". https://roostermoney.com/savings-pots-insights-2025/ · https://roostermoney.com/add-pots-to-customise-your-childs-dashboard/
- **Greenlight**: goals are tracked toward a "finish line", and "we'll cue the confetti" when the child reaches one. Level Up uses XP, coins, 3 stars per challenge, and you **lose a life** below 70% correct. https://greenlight.com/cash-back-savings-for-kids · https://help.greenlight.com/hc/en-us/articles/10412843038491-How-to-play-Greenlight-Level-Up
- **Revolut <18**: balance, spending and "Pockets" for goals with progress tracking. The visual format isn't described. https://www.revolut.com/en-US/kids-and-teens/kids-account/
- **Not verified:** the actual on-screen order (what sits at the top) for any of these. I found no case study or screenshot description that states it. BusyKid, Step, Acorns Early, Till, Monzo teen and Osper were not checked.

## 2. Gamified learning dashboards
- **Duolingo (2022 redesign)** turned the home screen into a single guided path. The stated reason: learners were unsure whether each step was "the best step". The next lesson leads and progress is the path itself. https://blog.duolingo.com/new-duolingo-home-screen-design/
- **Duolingo on its own streak** says it works through "loss aversion". It also admits that breaking a streak "can feel quite demotivating" and that "fear of losing a streak could prevent learners from even attempting one". Streak Freeze was added as slack. https://blog.duolingo.com/how-duolingo-streak-builds-habit/
- **Zogo** pays points ("pineapples") per module that convert to gift cards, and uses hearts. Reviews show learners chasing redemption thresholds rather than learning. https://apps.apple.com/us/app/zogo-learn-and-earn/id1474636588
- **Not checked:** Khan Academy Kids, Khanmigo and Prodigy.

## 3. Child safety and ethics
- **ICO Children's Code, Standard 5:** names "reward loops… notifications" as sticky features. It says to present options to continue "neutrally without suggesting that children will lose out", and to allow breaks "without losing their progress". Data-driven features that make disengaging hard are "likely to breach" the fairness principle. https://ico.org.uk/for-organisations/uk-gdpr-guidance-and-resources/childrens-information/childrens-code-guidance-and-resources/age-appropriate-design-a-code-of-practice-for-online-services/5-detrimental-use-of-data/
- **ICO Standard 13 (nudges):** pro-wellbeing nudges are allowed. As children get older, design should move toward "supporting them in developing conscious decision making skills". https://www.privacylaws.com/media/3093/final-age-appropriate-design-code-of-practice.pdf
- **Kuo, Zhao & Scott, IDC 2026** (50 parent–child pairs, children aged 6–12, Duolingo): children's goals drifted to "keeping the streak". They reported "streak panic" at bedtime and guilt from the mascot. The authors recommend replacing loss-framed streaks with progress based on mastery, keeping badges through gaps, a pause that isn't framed as loss, and characters with "no disappointment scripts". https://dl.acm.org/doi/full/10.1145/3773077.3806127
- **5Rights, *Disrupted Childhood*** lists streaks as a persuasive-design harm. https://5rightsfoundation.com/resource/updated-report-disrupted-childhood-the-cost-of-persuasive-design/

## 4. Goal visualisation research
- The goal-gradient effect is solid: effort rises as people near a goal. A CMU field study measured hourly progress of 6.6%, 10.7% and 12.7% across the three thirds of a goal. https://www.cmu.edu/dietrich/sds/docs/loewenstein/GoalGradBeh.pdf. Critics note the same effect can turn progress into obligation. https://medium.com/design-bootcamp/goal-gradient-effect-and-the-psychology-of-progress-bars-df6fd889fd8e
- **Not found:** any study comparing ring, bar and milestone road for children aged 11–14, and any study on "next step first" home screens for children. Duolingo's redesign is the only product evidence. Treat any shape choice as an untested design decision.

## Implications for the Jarso child Home
- **What leads:** one clear next step (today's mission or a pending inbox decision), as in Duolingo's path. Put the Spend and Save balances directly under it, because every money app above is built around the balance.
- **The goal:** make it the child's own, with a name and a photo or emoji (Rooster Money). Show amount saved out of target plus "N payments left" as plain counting, without a percentage pressure line. The shape is open since no evidence favours one. A bar or the jar filling is easiest to read. If the goal is complete or paused, show it in a calm, neutral way.
- **Learning progress:** show cumulative mastery (skills learned, badges kept forever), never a count of consecutive days. Pausing loses nothing (ICO Standard 5, IDC 2026 D2 and D4). The mascot never sounds sad.
- **Avoid:**
  - lives or hearts and losing them on a wrong answer (Greenlight)
  - points that convert to money or gift cards (Zogo; it also clashes with "never reward amount")
  - confetti on reaching a goal (Greenlight)
  - "don't lose out" copy on prompts to continue
  - notification loops driven by guilt

## Contradicted by the code

1. **"Replaces the current weekly learning-days count on child Home" (§6.1.3).** Child Home doesn't show that count. `daysThisWeek` is shown on `/child/learn` (`weeklyRhythmLine`) and on the parent's child learning page. So the doc's reason for the change targets a screen that doesn't have it.
2. **The plan doesn't prefill the handover (§5.3).** `docs/design.md` §6 says the handover sheet is "prefilled from the child's plan". Today it is prefilled from the last split (`prefillSplit(item.amountMinor, last)`), which matches the doc. The doc lists docs to update, but `design.md` isn't on that list.
3. **Navigation.** `design.md` §5 has the tabs Jars (child) and Requests (parent). The doc drops both. Also, §6 draws goal progress as a ring, and §7 gives `JarIllustration` a `fill` prop, not `capacity`, `filled` and `reserved`. Again, `design.md` isn't on the update list.
4. **"Gift occurrences" (§4.1).** Gifts aren't occurrences. They live in a separate table, `parent_gifts`, with `give_on` and statuses `pending`, `given` and `cancelled`. RLS hides them from the child completely. So gifts must never reach the child's `allowance_due` notice or the child's "due money" coin. The doc doesn't say this.
5. **"The clock helper" (§4.1).** There isn't a single helper. Time functions take a `p_now` parameter or have an `_at` variant, and use `family_today(family_id, p_now)`. This is workable, not blocking.
6. **Slice 1 hasn't landed.** There is no `requests` table and no `reserved_save()`. The notification kinds for requests exist, but nothing writes them yet. The doc assumes this order, so it's not wrong, just not done yet.

## Questions, most important first

1. **Where does everything else on child Home go?** Home today also shows the "Ask the money guide" sheet, `PendingPurchases` (the offline outbox, PR #33), the purchase history with Undo, and `ChildAllowanceCard`, which carries the schedule and any pause message to the child. The new Home lists only the next step, the jars and the skills strip.
   - The doc says history is "reachable from the Spend jar", but the child has no history route. It lives only on Home.
   - Getting this wrong either loses existing features (the guide entry, Undo, offline status, the pause message) or creates a new route nobody designed.
2. **Is dropping roadmap slice 5 scope intended?** The roadmap says "goal as a journey with milestones", "learning days this week" and "Designed in Figma first". The doc has no milestones, removes the days count, and uses an HTML mockup instead of Figma. This decides whether the plan includes milestone work and whether a Figma step blocks it.
3. **Which "today" ends the weekly count?** If the days count should go everywhere, including `/child/learn` and the parent's learning page, that touches shipped screens and tests. If it should stay there, the "replace" applies only to the new skills strip.
4. **"£20 equivalent" in the shared fill scale.** Currency can be any ISO code, and some have different minor-unit sizes (JPY has none). Does this mean converting at an exchange rate, a fixed floor per currency, or simply 20 major units? Conversion would need a rate source. The other options are a constant.
5. **Do pending requests appear in the child's "Do this next"?** Roadmap slice 3 says "goal and pending requests in the next step on both homes". The doc's order has only an approved withdrawal. This changes the unit-tested next-step order.
6. **Which occurrence states get reminders?** Should reminders continue when the schedule is paused or stopped but an old occurrence is still `due`? And should occurrences already overdue when this ships be backfilled, or only new ones? A wrong guess either sends notices the demo didn't expect or misses the demo's own due item.
7. **Plan wording on the parent card.** "plans £4 a week" conflicts with "about N more allowances whatever the frequency". Schedules can also be monthly, quarterly or annual. Should the parent card use the schedule's frequency word or "each time"?

select count(*)::int as a_total from public.notifications where recipient_id = :'pa'::uuid \gset

select tests.authenticate_as(:'pa'::uuid);
select is(public.unread_notification_count(), :a_total, 'unread count before');
select is(public.mark_notifications_read(array[:'a_row'::uuid]), 1, 'marks own row');
select is(public.mark_notifications_read(array[:'b_row'::uuid]), 0, 'cannot mark another recipient''s row');
select is(public.unread_notification_count(), :a_total - 1, 'unread count after one');
select is(public.mark_notifications_read(null), :a_total - 1, 'null marks all remaining own rows');
select is(public.unread_notification_count(), 0, 'unread count zero');
select throws_ok($$update public.notifications set read_at = null$$, '42501', null, 'direct update denied');
select tests.clear_auth();
select is((select count(*)::int from public.notifications where recipient_id = :'pb'::uuid and read_at is not null), 0, 'other parent unread state unchanged');

select * from finish();
rollback;

timestamp: 2026-10-30T16:52:08.192Z
time: 2026-10-30 16:52
chars: 3950

### Spec Compliance

- ✅ Check constraints (`inbox_reminders.sql:4-12`). Drop and recreate keep all 5 existing kinds and both existing `source_type` values, and add `allowance_due`, `handover_reminder` and `allowance_occurrence`.
- ✅ `send_handover_reminders` (`:21-62`).
  - It filters on `status = 'due'` (Review Focus 1).
  - `d` is computed from family-local `family_today(family_id, p_now)`, so the 3-day rule has no UTC drift.
  - `d = 0` notifies parents and the child with window `id:due`.
  - `d > 0 and d % 3 = 0` notifies parents only, with window `id:<local date>`.
  - The payload is `{amount_minor, due_on, days_overdue}`.
  - It returns the sum of insert counts.
  - Late first sight waits for the next 3-day mark.
  - Paused schedules still remind, because there is no schedule filter.
- ✅ Grants. Execute is revoked from `public, anon, authenticated`, and the test confirms `authenticated` gets 42501. `service_role` keeps the default grant.
- ✅ Cron job `send-handover-reminders` at `7 * * * *` (`:66-78`). It matches the `generate-due-occurrences` pattern exactly: `pg_available_extensions` guard, warning fallback, same `statement_timeout` command.
- ✅ `mark_notifications_read` and `unread_notification_count`.
  - Both are security definer with `search_path = ''` and are granted to `authenticated` only.
  - Scoping is by `auth.uid()`, so another parent's rows are untouched (Review Focus 2).
  - There is no update grant on `notifications`, and the test checks that a direct update is denied.
- ✅ No app-side inserts, no email, and no gift code touched.
- ✅ Test coverage (`inbox_reminders.test.sql`) covers every brief bullet. Types and `docs/database.md` were updated.
- ⚠️ I did not re-run the suite. The report says all 27 assertions pass after `db:reset`. I could not check that `notify_parents` and `notify_child` return row counts, or that they dedupe via `on conflict do nothing`. The tests imply both: reruns return 0.
- ⚠️ I could not verify the types file, because it was excluded from the diff.

### Strengths
- Date math uses family-local dates, and idempotency is enforced by the DB unique index on `window_key`. Overlapping cron runs and hourly reruns are therefore safe.
- The tests use fixed `p_now` values and assert both counts and recipients. They cover the late-first-sight case, the paused case, the confirmed-between-runs case, and the other parent's unread state.
- The only change to existing objects is a constraint recreation, and it is correct.

### Issues

**Critical:** none.

**Important:** none.

**Minor**
1. `inbox_reminders.sql:18` and `:21`. The header comment says "One bad row must not stop the rest", but the loop has no `exception` block. A failure in `notify_*` would abort the whole run. Either wrap each iteration in `begin ... exception when others` (as `generate_due_occurrences` does with `v_failed`) or delete the comment. For about 60 users this is low risk, but the comment is misleading. Also, no `job_runs` row is written, so there is no observability. The report acknowledged this.
2. `inbox_reminders.test.sql:67-68`. The "gifts untouched" assertion is vacuous because no `parent_gifts` row exists. Insert a pending gift due today and assert it produces no notice, which is what invariant 17 requires.
3. `inbox_reminders.sql:107-114`. `unread_notification_count` filters on `is_approved_member`, but `mark_notifications_read` does not. A removed member's rows can still be marked, which is harmless. The docs note the difference, so this is consistency only.
4. The test does not cover a `closed` occurrence. It would be a cheap addition, but `status = 'due'` already covers it.

### Assessment
**Task quality: Approved.** The migration matches the brief on constraints, reminder timing, grants, cron guard and read-state isolation. The tests exercise real behaviour. Only minor items remain: a misleading comment, a vacuous gift assertion, and no per-row error isolation.

The coordinator sent a message while you were working:
Task 2 review: needs fixes. Fix these, re-run the covering tests (src/features/notifications), run `pnpm check` once, commit (e.g. `fix(notifications): decided payload key and stable inbox cursor`), append a fix report (changes, tests, command, output) to /tmp/jarso-sdd/inbox-goal-jar-home/task-2-report.md, and reply with the short status contract.

1. (Important) links.ts decided notices read `payload.decision`, but the slice 4 plan writes `notify_child('withdrawal_decided', payload {approved})` — a boolean `approved`. Read `typeof payload.approved === "boolean"` (you may keep `decision` as a fallback). Add tests: approved → "said yes" copy, declined → "not now" copy, unknown/missing → generic fallback, for both withdrawal_decided and deposit_decided.
2. (Important) Pagination cursor is `created_at` only with `lt`, while ordering is `(created_at desc, id desc)`. Notices from one reminder run share `created_at`, so rows get skipped at a page boundary. Use a compound `(created_at, id)` cursor (e.g. `nextBefore = "<iso>|<id>"`, filter `or(created_at.lt.X,and(created_at.eq.X,id.lt.Y))`); validate/parse the cursor safely. Test that a boundary inside a same-timestamp group does not skip rows (at the service/repository seam you can test).
3. (Spec gap) The brief asks that `listNotices` "maps rows to Notice" is tested; the mapping lives in repository.ts and is untested. Extract the row→Notice mapping as a pure function (same file is fine) and unit-test it: null child join, non-object/array payload, read_at → read.
4. `noticeText` switch has no default: an unexpected kind from the DB returns undefined and would crash the UI. Add a safe fallback `{ title: "Something new", detail: "Take a look." }` with a test.

Address this before completing your current task.

- `noticeHref` and `noticeText` are pure, and `noticeHref` is a single exhaustive branch.
- The repository is the only place with table, column and RPC names. It guards against a non-object `payload` and a missing joined child.
- `noticeText` degrades when an amount is missing: the double-space `replace` and the null-checked fallbacks all produce sensible strings.
- Layering and barrels are right: `server-only` split, the action is not in the client barrel, and auth goes through `assertUser`.

## Issues

**Important**

1. `links.ts:208-215`, decided notices read the wrong payload key. The code checks `payload.decision === "approved" | "declined"`, but the slice 4 plan (Tasks 9/10) specifies `{approved: boolean}`. Every real `withdrawal_decided` and `deposit_decided` notice would show the generic "Your parent answered your request", never "said yes" or "said not now". This is cross-plan; no DB code writes these notices yet.
   - Fix: read `typeof payload.approved === "boolean"`, optionally keeping `decision` as a fallback.
   - Add a test for approved, declined and unknown payloads.

2. `repository.ts:287` with `service.ts:403`, the pagination cursor is `created_at` only, with `lt`.
   - The ordering is `(created_at desc, id desc)`, but the cursor drops `id`.
   - Notices written in one transaction or batch (reminder runs, multiple children) share a `created_at` via `now()`. When a page boundary falls inside such a group, the remaining rows with the same timestamp are skipped. They never appear in the inbox.
   - Fix: use a compound cursor on `(created_at, id)`, for example `nextBefore = "<ts>|<id>"` with `or(created_at.lt.X,and(created_at.eq.X,id.lt.Y))`. Alternatively, use `lte` and de-duplicate by id.

**Minor**

3. `links.ts:170`, the switch has no default. A `kind` string from the DB that is not in the union, cast at `repository.ts:292`, makes `noticeText` return `undefined`. Destructuring `title` then throws in the UI. Add a safe fallback such as `{ title: "Something new", detail: "Take a look." }`.

4. `service.test.ts` and `links.test.ts` have coverage gaps:
   - There is no test of the repository mapping: null child, array payload, `read_at` to `read`.
   - There is no `noticeText` test for the decided, requested or `deposit_*` kinds.
   - There is no test for a missing or non-numeric amount, or a JPY currency.
   - The "full page returns the cursor" test only restates the implementation, and it would not catch issue 2.

5. `links.ts:185`, `days_overdue: 0` yields the detail "0 days". It is also not guarded against a negative value. Cosmetic.

6. `links.ts:208-216` serves both decided kinds with child-voiced copy whatever the role. This is fine while decided notices are child-only, but `role` is ignored. Worth a comment.

## Assessment
**Task quality: Needs fixes.** The structure, layering and fixed copy are correct. Fix the decided-payload key (issue 1) and the `(created_at, id)` cursor (issue 2) before this is relied on.

chars: 2807

Per-finding verdicts
1. ADDRESSED. `src/features/notifications/links.ts:~82-91` now reads `payload.approved` (true gives "said yes", false gives "said not now"), and keeps the legacy `decision` string as a fallback. `links.test.ts` (the new `it.each`) covers approved, declined and unknown (`{}`) for both `withdrawal_decided` and `deposit_decided`.
2. ADDRESSED. `repository.ts` (`listNoticeRows`) uses `or(created_at.lt."T",and(created_at.eq."T",id.lt.ID))`, which matches `order created_at desc, id desc`. `service.ts:14` builds the cursor as `encodeCursor(last)`, i.e. `<created_at>|<id>`. Tests: `service.test.ts` has a same-timestamp case checking that `nextBefore` carries the id. `repository.test.ts` checks that the cursor round-trips with microseconds kept and rejects junk and filter injection. The `or()` string itself is not exercised by any test (no mocked builder), but I checked it by reading the library source (see the or() check).
3. ADDRESSED. `toNotice` is exported from `repository.ts` and tested in `repository.test.ts` for a plain row, a null child join (gives `""`), payloads that are an array, a string or null (all give `{}`), and `read_at` set (gives `read: true`).
4. ADDRESSED. `links.ts` has a `default:` branch returning "Something new" / "Take a look.", and `links.test.ts` tests it with an unknown kind.

or() filter check (the named risk): no problem.
- postgrest-js 2.118.0 `or()` calls `url.searchParams.append('or', '(' + filters + ')')`. URLSearchParams percent-encodes `+` to `%2B`, and `:` and `,` are safe once quoted.
- The timestamps are wrapped in double quotes inside the PostgREST logic tree, so `.`, `:` and `+` are not treated as operators or separators. A `+00:00` offset therefore reaches Postgres intact and parses as timestamptz.
- Microsecond precision is kept because the cursor is a string passed through unchanged. Using `eq` on the timestamp is safe for that reason.
- `CURSOR_RE` accepts `Z` and `+hh[:mm]` offsets, so PostgREST's own `+00:00` output matches. The uuid and timestamp are regex-validated, so injection is blocked, and the test confirms it.

New breakage: none Critical or Important.

Minor (deferred)
- `listNoticeRows` has no test of the generated `or()` string or of the real PostgREST round trip. Consider an e2e or integration check once an inbox page consumes the cursor.
- No code consumes `nextBefore` yet. When a client page or route passes it through a URL, it must be URL-encoded. An unencoded `+` becomes a space, and `CURSOR_RE` would then reject the cursor with a VALIDATION error. Safest is to pass it via `searchParams` or `encodeURIComponent`.
- `row as Row` in `listNoticeRows` is a cast over the generated types. It is harmless but loosens type checking if the select string drifts.

Verdict: all findings addressed

### Assessment

### Spec Compliance
- ✅ `InboxItem` (`inbox-item.tsx:50-86`): tinted icon circle, title, detail, relative time and chevron. `data-unread` and `bg-teal-100` are set on unread rows. `min-h-11` gives a 44 px target. Unread is also shown by bold text plus an sr-only "(new)", not by colour alone.
- ✅ `InboxList` (`inbox-list.tsx`): newest first, "Mark all as read" outline button (`ids: null`), "Show more", and the "Nothing here yet." empty state. Mark-all shows a toast on failure.
- ✅ Pages call `requireRole` inside `<Suspense>` and `searchParams` is awaited in the inner component, so rule 6 holds. Zod parses `?before=`.
- ✅ Nav: Inbox added to the child nav (Home, Learn, Inbox) and the parent nav (Home, Allowance, Inbox). The unread count reaches `BottomNav.count` as a plain number prop.
- ✅ Barrel exports, `playwright.config.ts` project, e2e flow matching Step 4.

- ❌ The layout part of Step 3 ("so the layout never awaits…") is met only by making the whole nav wait on the count (see Important 1).
- ⚠️ I did not run the e2e spec or check the badge's gold fill and 20 px minimum. That depends on `Badge variant="count"`, which is outside the diff.

### Strengths
- Modified clicks (meta, ctrl, shift, middle button) bypass the interception, so open-in-new-tab still works.
- The `?before=` cursor round trip is sound. `encodeURIComponent` turns `+` into `%2B`, `searchParams` decodes it, and `CURSOR_RE` accepts `…+00:00|uuid`. The length cap of 100 fits, and the page param is `.catch`-guarded against array or non-string values.
- Layout error handling uses `unstable_rethrow`, with a count-less nav as the fallback.
- Parent and child pages duplicate each other but stay simple.

### Issues

**Important**
1. **Tab bar hidden until the count resolves** (`child/layout.tsx:19-23`, `parent/layout.tsx:15-17`). `ChildNavWithCount` awaits `getUnreadCount()` (a Supabase call) inside `<Suspense fallback={null}>`. On a slow network the tab bar is missing entirely until that call returns, which regresses the shell, and it goes missing on error paths too.
   - Why it matters: the nav used to depend only on `usePathname`.
   - Fix: a count-less fallback is feasible. Nest the boundaries so the fallback can read the pathname safely:
     ```tsx
     <Suspense fallback={null}>
       <Suspense fallback={<ChildNav />}>
         <ChildNavWithCount />
       </Suspense>
     </Suspense>
     ```
     The outer boundary absorbs the pathname suspension on param routes. The inner fallback shows the tabs immediately, and the count fills in when it arrives.
2. **Navigation depends on the mark-read action succeeding** (`inbox-list.tsx:30-35`). `open()` calls `preventDefault`, then awaits `markNoticesReadAction` before `router.push`.
   - **Waits:** every notice open costs a full server-action round trip before anything happens.
   - **Failure:** `createAction` converts server errors into `{ok:false}`, which is ignored here, so those cases still navigate silently. That is acceptable.
   - **Offline:** a network failure makes the action call reject. The rejection is unhandled inside `startTransition`, so React sends it to the nearest error boundary and `router.push` never runs. An offline or flaky user taps a notice and gets an error page instead of the record, which defeats the PR #33 offline support.
   - Fix: use `try { await …; if (!result.ok) logger… } catch {}` with `finally { router.push(href) }`, or fire the mark without awaiting it and navigate straight away. Marking read is best effort. The destination screen is not blocked on it.

**Minor**
3. **Bad cursor does not fall back** (`child/inbox/page.tsx:12,22`, same in the parent page). The comment says "anything unusable means the newest page", but Zod only checks the type and length. A malformed `?before=x` reaches `parseCursor`, which throws `AppError VALIDATION`, so the user gets an error page. Fix: validate with the cursor regex in the schema, or catch the error and drop the cursor.
4. **Badge may be stale until a refresh** (⚠️ not verified). The count lives in a persistent layout and is only re-read when the layout is re-rendered. The action calls `revalidatePath` only on the two inbox paths. The e2e step "badge gone" probably covers the main case. Worth confirming that the count refreshes after mark-read when you navigate away from `/inbox`.
5. **Duplicated code.** The two pages and the two nav-with-count wrappers are near copies; fine for now.

### Assessment
**Task quality: Needs fixes.** The feature matches the brief, but the open-then-navigate path in `InboxList` can strand the user on an error page when the network is down. The tab bar also now waits on a DB read before it renders.

### Spec Compliance
- ✅ `InboxItem` (`src/features/notifications/components/inbox-item.tsx:50-86`): tinted icon circle, title, detail, relative time and chevron. `data-unread` and `bg-teal-100` are set on unread rows. `min-h-11` gives a 44 px target. Unread is also shown by bold text plus an sr-only "(new)", not by colour alone.
- ✅ `InboxList` (`inbox-list.tsx`): newest first, "Mark all as read" outline button (`ids: null`), "Show more", and the "Nothing here yet." empty state. Mark-all shows a toast on failure.
- ✅ Both inbox pages call `requireRole` inside `<Suspense>`, and `searchParams` is awaited in the inner component, so rule 6 holds. Zod parses `?before=`.
- ✅ Nav: Inbox added to the child nav (Home, Learn, Inbox) and the parent nav (Home, Allowance, Inbox). The unread count reaches `BottomNav.count` as a plain number prop.
- ✅ Barrel exports, the `playwright.config.ts` project, and an e2e flow matching Step 4.
- ✅ The `viewer` prop name instead of `role` is harmless.
- ❌ The layout part of Step 3 ("so the layout never awaits…") is met only by making the whole nav wait on the count (see Important 1).
- ⚠️ I did not run the e2e spec or check the badge's gold fill and 20 px minimum. That depends on `Badge variant="count"`, which is outside the diff.

### Strengths
- Modified clicks (meta, ctrl, shift, middle button) bypass the interception, so open-in-new-tab still works.
- The `?before=` cursor round trip is sound. `encodeURIComponent` turns `+` into `%2B`, `searchParams` decodes it, and `CURSOR_RE` accepts `…+00:00|uuid`. The length cap of 100 fits, and the page param is `.catch`-guarded against array or non-string values.
- Layout error handling uses `unstable_rethrow`, with a count-less nav as the error fallback.

### Issues

**Important**
1. **Tab bar hidden until the count resolves** (`src/app/(app)/child/layout.tsx:19-23`, `src/app/(app)/parent/layout.tsx:15-17`). `ChildNavWithCount` and `ParentNavWithCount` await `getUnreadCount()` (a Supabase call) inside `<Suspense fallback={null}>`. On a slow network the tab bar is missing entirely until that call returns, which regresses the shell. Before this change the nav depended only on `usePathname`.
   - Fix: a count-less fallback is feasible. Nest the boundaries so the fallback can read the pathname safely:
     ```tsx
     <Suspense fallback={null}>
       <Suspense fallback={<ChildNav />}>
         <ChildNavWithCount />
       </Suspense>
     </Suspense>
     ```
     The outer boundary absorbs the pathname suspension on param routes. The inner fallback shows the tabs immediately, and the count fills in when it arrives.
2. **Navigation depends on the mark-read action succeeding** (`src/features/notifications/components/inbox-list.tsx:30-35`). `open()` calls `preventDefault`, then awaits `markNoticesReadAction` before `router.push`.
   - **Waits:** every notice open costs a full server-action round trip before anything happens.
   - **Failure:** `createAction` converts server errors into `{ok:false}`, which is ignored here, so those cases still navigate silently. That is acceptable.
   - **Offline:** a network failure makes the action call reject. The rejection is unhandled inside `startTransition`, so React sends it to the nearest error boundary and `router.push` never runs. An offline or flaky user taps a notice and gets an error page instead of the record, which defeats the PR #33 offline support.
   - Fix: wrap the call in try/catch (log the failure) and push the route in `finally`, or fire the mark without awaiting it and navigate straight away. Marking read is best effort. The destination screen should not be blocked on it.

**Minor**
3. **Bad cursor does not fall back** (`src/app/(app)/child/inbox/page.tsx:12-17`, same in `src/app/(app)/parent/inbox/page.tsx`). The comment says "anything unusable means the newest page", but Zod only checks the type and length. A malformed `?before=x` reaches `parseCursor` in `server/repository.ts`, which throws `AppError VALIDATION`, so the user gets an error page. Fix: validate with the cursor regex in the schema, or catch the error and drop the cursor.
4. **Badge may be stale until a refresh** (⚠️ not verified). The count lives in a persistent layout and is only re-read when the layout re-renders. The action calls `revalidatePath` only on the two inbox paths. The e2e step "badge gone" probably covers the main case. Worth confirming that the count refreshes after mark-read when you navigate away from `/inbox`.
5. **Duplicated code.** The two pages and the two nav-with-count wrappers are near copies; acceptable for now.

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=SUBAGENT_RESPONSE num=1 session=14c1a4e8-25b8-4869-859a-f799b43c74df agent=a431fdfa87dd187ec]
timestamp: 2026-10-30T17:08:03.218Z
time: 2026-10-30 17:08
chars: 3487

**Per-finding verdicts**

1. ADDRESSED. `src/app/(app)/child/layout.tsx:19-22` and `src/app/(app)/parent/layout.tsx:14-17` nest `<Suspense fallback={<ChildNav />}>` and `<Suspense fallback={<ParentNav />}>` inside the existing `fallback={null}` outer boundary.
   - The count-less bar now shows while the count loads.
   - The pathname constraint holds. `BottomNav` calls `usePathname()` (`src/components/shell/bottom-nav.tsx:33`). On param routes that call suspends during prerender, and it suspends inside the fallback, which sits under the outer boundary. The outer boundary absorbs it, as before.
   - I did not run a build.

2. PARTLY ADDRESSED. `src/features/notifications/components/inbox-list.tsx:35-42` calls `router.push` synchronously and no longer awaits the action.
   - A rejection is caught and logged (`.catch`), so navigation always happens.
   - The test at `inbox-list.test.tsx` ("still navigates when saving read fails") covers it.
   - The defect is the `.finally(() => router.refresh())` on the failure path. See New breakage.

3. ADDRESSED. `src/features/notifications/schemas.ts:6,10-14` adds `inboxParamsSchema`. It uses a strict `<ts>|<uuid>` regex plus `.catch(undefined)`. Both inbox pages use it.
   - `schemas.test.ts` covers `"x"`, `""`, a date only, an array, a number, `"a|b"`, a real cursor and repeated params.

4. ADDRESSED on the success path. `router.refresh()` at `inbox-list.tsx:41` runs after the action settles. It re-renders the layout, so the count updates wherever the user landed. The action's `revalidatePath` also helps.

**New breakage**

- **Important: `router.refresh()` runs after a failed mark-read, which is the offline case.** It is at `inbox-list.tsx:41`, in `.finally`.
  - The app has no service worker (no `serviceWorker`, serwist or workbox references in `src`, `public`, `next.config.*` or `package.json`).
  - The refresh needs a fresh RSC fetch. When that fetch fails, Next's `fetch-server-response.js:199-229` logs `Failed to fetch RSC payload...` and falls back to a hard browser navigation. The offline-wait retry only runs if `__NEXT_USE_OFFLINE` is set, and I did not confirm it is.
  - So the push can land on a prefetched page, then the refresh can trigger a full reload of that page while offline. That defeats the offline fix.
  - Fix: refresh only on success, for example `.then(() => router.refresh(), (e) => logger.warn(...))`.
  - Change the second test to assert `refresh` is not called on rejection. As written it asserts the opposite and locks in the bug.
- **Named risk, refresh firing after the user has navigated: acceptable.** `router.refresh()` is called once per click, from a click handler, so there is no loop. It refreshes whichever route is current when it fires. The effect is a soft re-render of the landing page, which is the intent (finding 4). I found no jarring case beyond the offline one above.
- **Named risk, `logger` in a client component: safe.** `src/lib/logger.ts` is isomorphic. It reads `process.env.LOG_LEVEL` and `NODE_ENV`, which Next inlines or leaves undefined on the client, and it only writes to `console.warn`. It logs just the error name, so there is no PII.

**Minor (deferred)**

- `src/features/notifications/schemas.ts:7` has a stray leftover regex literal, a no-op expression statement. Biome passes, but delete it.
- The `CURSOR` regex's `[0-9a-f-]{36}` is looser than a UUID. Harmless, because the repository still validates the value.

Verdict: open findings remain

Finding 1 (refresh only on mark-read success; navigation unaffected): NOT ADDRESSED (partial).
- Rejection path fixed: src/features/notifications/components/inbox-list.tsx:35-41 now uses `.then(onOk, onErr)`, so refresh no longer runs on rejection. Navigation at :43 is still synchronous and does not await the action.
- Resolved-but-failed path still refreshes: :36 `() => router.refresh()` runs for any resolved value, including `{ ok: false }`. The spec says refresh only when the action succeeds, so this is open. Fix: `(result) => { if (result.ok) router.refresh(); }`.
- Tests: src/features/notifications/components/inbox-list.test.tsx:37-44 asserts refresh IS called on success (ok). :46-53 asserts refresh is NOT called on rejection (ok; setTimeout 0 is enough for the rejection handler to settle). No test covers a resolved `{ ok: false }` case, so the gap above is untested.

Finding 2 (stray regex statement removed): ADDRESSED. src/features/notifications/schemas.ts:1-13 no longer has the bare regex line. Only the CURSOR const at :6 remains, and the removed line was a no-op expression.

Question: does createAction resolve on server errors? Yes. src/lib/action.ts:49-68 wraps the handler in try/catch. AppError and unknown errors go through toFailure (:14-26, :67) and resolve `{ ok: false, error: {...} }`. Only Next control-flow errors are rethrown via unstable_rethrow (:66). So the `.then` success handler also fires on `{ ok: false }`, which is the gap above.
Is it a real problem? Low impact. The app is online, the refresh is harmless (no data loss, no wrong badge beyond a re-render), and it costs one extra round trip. It is still a spec mismatch, so fix it and add a test for it.

New breakage: none Critical or Important. The removed schemas.ts line was a no-op. The success handler has no catch, so if router.refresh() ever threw it would surface as an unhandled rejection (Minor, not introduced as a regression). I did not run vitest, biome or tsc; this review was read-only. Verdict is from source reading and the diff.

Verdict: open findings remain

## Final review: PR C, inbox and handover reminders (831e6fb..684b56b, feat/inbox-reminders)

Ran myself: `pnpm check` passes (Biome shows 2 warnings, both in `learning/server/service.test.ts` and both from before this PR; typecheck clean; 769/769 unit tests). `supabase test db`: `inbox_reminders.test.sql` passes. `notifications.test.sql` fails 3 tests on the local DB (see Minor 5).

### Declined to judge
- **`or()` cursor filter against real PostgREST:** I tried to run it against local Supabase with a small script and the permission was denied. It is still unverified (see Important 3).
- **Hosted DB / production cron state:** I can't reach it from here.
- **Visual contrast of unread rows (teal-100 and the dark variant) and the badge in a browser:** I read code only and took no screenshots.
- **Inbox e2e:** I didn't re-run it. Playwright builds the production app, so a pass there also covers Cache Components prerender.

### Strengths
- **SQL hygiene is good.** Every new function is `security definer` with `set search_path = ''` and qualified names. `send_handover_reminders` is revoked from public, anon and authenticated. The read-state functions are granted to `authenticated` only, and `notifications` still has no update grant (there is a pgTAP test for that).
- **Reminders reuse `notify_parents` / `notify_child`.** Fan-out goes only to approved members, and dedupe uses the existing unique window index.
- **Gifts can't leak.** The job reads only `allowance_occurrences`, so `parent_gifts` structurally cannot produce a notice. The child gets only `allowance_due`, never reminders. Invariant 17 holds.
- **Review Focus 1 and 2 are both tested directly** (handed over between runs; two parents with independent read state). The late-first-sight and paused-schedule cases are tested too.
- **Integration is consistent end to end.** SQL kinds and sources match `NoticeKind`/`NoticeSource`, `kindStyle` (a `Record<NoticeKind,…>`, checked by the type system), `noticeText` and `noticeHref`.
- **The cursor works across all three layers.**
  - It is a `(created_at, id)` compound that keeps microseconds.
  - `parseCursor` rejects filter injection.
  - The page schema `.catch(undefined)` falls back to the newest page.
- **Badge path is solid.**
  - Nested Suspense, so the tab bar renders without a count while it loads or on error, and `unstable_rethrow` is used.
  - The layout never awaits the session at its top level.
  - Opening a notice saves "read" as best effort and then navigates. I checked Next 16 `app-router-instance.js`: a server action dropped by a navigation still resolves, and its `didRevalidate` causes a refresh, so the badge clears.
- **Docs match the email-later decision** (roadmap, first-milestone supersede notes, api.md).

### Issues

**Critical:** none.

**Important**

1. **"Mark all as read" sends the user to the error page when offline or the connection drops.** `src/features/notifications/components/inbox-list.tsx:48-53`
   - **What:** `markAll` awaits `markNoticesReadAction` inside an async `startTransition` with no catch. Offline, the server action rejects with a TypeError. React 19 sends errors thrown inside a transition to the nearest error boundary, so a child tapping the button on a bad connection loses the page.
   - **Why it matters:** `docs/architecture.md:604` sets the convention that online-only actions go through `runOnline` (`@/features/offline`), and about 20 call sites follow it.
   - **Fix:** `const result = await runOnline(() => markNoticesReadAction(null, { ids: null }));`. The existing `toast.error(result.error.message)` then shows the calm offline or slow copy.

2. **The badge goes stale during in-app navigation, which contradicts spec §4.1.** `src/app/(app)/child/layout.tsx:21,32`, `src/app/(app)/parent/layout.tsx:16,24`
   - **What:** Spec §4.1 (line 71) says the count "refreshes when the user navigates". The count lives in a layout, and layouts don't re-render on client navigation (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/05-config/01-next-config-js/staleTimes.md:35`). It updates only on a full load, `router.refresh()`, or a server action that revalidates.
   - **Effect:** If a parent has the tab open when the cron or a child's over-limit purchase creates a notice, the badge doesn't move until they reload or do something. Worse, they can open `/parent/inbox`, see unread rows, and the badge still shows the old count. The e2e misses this because it uses `page.goto`.
   - **Fix (pick one):**
     - Cheapest: a small client effect in the nav that calls `router.refresh()` on `visibilitychange` to visible and when the pathname enters an inbox route.
     - Or accept the behaviour and change the spec/fr-audit wording to "refreshes on load and after reading".
   - Either way the gap should be decided, not left silent.

3. **Inbox pagination has never run against PostgREST** (deferred T2 item: **verify before merge**). `src/features/notifications/server/repository.ts:62-66`
   - **What:** The quoted `created_at.lt."…+00:00"` inside `or()` is covered only by mocks.
   - **Why it matters:** A parent gets 20+ notices in about 2–3 months (a weekly due notice plus a reminder every 3 days). If the filter is wrong, "Show more" throws and lands on the error page.
   - **Fix:** Either click "Show more" once by hand against local Supabase, or seed 21 notices in the inbox e2e through the admin client and assert that page 2 renders with no duplicates.

**Minor**

1. **The migration comment promises behaviour the code doesn't have** (deferred T1 item: **fix the comment before merge; add `job_runs` as a follow-up**). `supabase/migrations/20261030164347_inbox_reminders.sql:17`
   - It says "One bad row must not stop the rest", but there is no per-row exception block and no `job_runs` row, unlike `generate_due_occurrences`.
   - The practical risk is low, since the notify calls are `on conflict do nothing` and a null `family_today` is skipped.
   - Either reword the comment, or copy the generator's per-row block and `job_runs` row.
   - Add "reminder job has no `job_runs` row" to the fr-audit follow-ups next to "Generator timeout".

2. **fr-audit production state is inaccurate** (deferred T4 item: **fix before merge**). `docs/fr-audit.md:32,34`
   - "Pending until `pnpm db:push` after merge" contradicts the same row and `docs/deploy.md:82`: the GitHub integration applies migrations on merge, and `db:push` is only the fallback.
   - The counts are wrong. The repo has 28 migrations (27 on main), not "20 in the repo, 19 applied". Drop the counts or correct them.
   - The `send-handover-reminders` row should say it is created when the migration is applied on merge.

3. **The acceptance criteria row understates progress.** `docs/fr-audit.md:50`: "Parent gets in-app notices for request and limit breach | No" is now "Partly" (limit notices show in the inbox; request notices need slice 4). The tally becomes 9 met and 2 partly met, in fr-audit and in roadmap.md.

4. **Copy edge cases.** `src/features/notifications/links.ts:40,57`
   - When the child profile join returns null (for example after `remove_child`), `childName` is "" and the title renders "'s US$5 allowance is due". Fall back to "Your child".
   - The parent reminder detail "6 days" is cryptic on its own. "Due 6 days ago" reads better and still fits §9.

5. **Database tests depend on global DB state.**
   - `inbox_reminders.test.sql` asserts on `send_handover_reminders` return values, and that function scans every `due` occurrence in the DB. Leftover e2e data with a due occurrence on a 3-day mark would break it locally.
   - Related: the older `notifications.test.sql` uses unscoped `count(*)`. It now fails locally (3/22, observed) once the new cron or the inbox e2e has written rows (the local DB has 6 `allowance_due` rows).
   - CI resets the DB first, so CI is green.
   - Fix: scope the counts to the test's own users and occurrences. A follow-up is fine.

6. **"Gifts untouched" test is vacuous** (deferred T1 item: **cheap, recommended, not blocking**). `supabase/tests/database/inbox_reminders.test.sql:64-65`
   - No `parent_gifts` row exists, so the assertion can't fail.
   - Insert a pending gift with `give_on` = the due day and assert that no notice gets created. Invariant 17 is a stated review focus, so a test that can fail is worth having.

7. **The empty state is missing the mark illustration and an action** required by design.md §6 ("Mark illustration, one sentence, one action"). `inbox-list.tsx:56-60` uses `InboxIcon` and has no action. Polish; it can wait for PR E.

**Other deferred items: leave all of these.**
- T1: `mark_notifications_read` has no `is_approved_member` filter. It only touches the caller's own rows, so it is harmless.
- T1: no test for a closed occurrence. It uses the same `status = 'due'` predicate as the confirmed case, which is tested.
- T2: `days_overdue` 0 renders "0 days". Unreachable, because reminders fire only for d > 0.
- T2: the decided copy ignores role. Only the child gets decided notices.
- T2: `noticeText` has no tests for the requested kinds, a missing amount, or JPY. Nice to have.
- T2: `row as Row` cast in the repository.
- T3: the two inbox pages and the two nav wrappers are near-duplicates. PR E rebuilds these.
- T3: the CURSOR regex is looser than a uuid. The repository validates it again.
- T3: a mark-read that returns `ok:false` is silent. It is best effort and the navigation still happens.

### Verdict
Ready to merge: **With fixes.**

Before merge:
- Route "Mark all as read" through `runOnline` (Important 1).
- Settle the badge staleness, either with a small refresh or by correcting the spec wording (Important 2).
- Exercise "Show more" against real PostgREST once (Important 3).
- Fix the fr-audit production-state rows and the misleading migration comment (Minor 1–2).

The SQL, RLS, gift secrecy and the cross-layer contract are sound.

**Per-item verdicts**

1. ADDRESSED. `inbox-list.tsx:49` wraps the action in `runOnline`. The unit test in `inbox-list.test.tsx` rejects the action with a TypeError and asserts `toast.error` with offline copy. `runOnline` converts both a rejected action and `navigator.onLine === false` into `{ok:false}`, so the existing toast path handles it.
2. ADDRESSED.
   - `src/components/shell/inbox-refresh.tsx` is rendered in `src/app/(app)/layout.tsx` inside `<Suspense fallback={null}>`.
   - It refreshes on `visibilitychange` to visible, and on entering `/child/inbox` or `/parent/inbox` (not on first render). It skips both when `navigator.onLine === false`.
   - Unit tests cover all of those conditions. See "New breakage" for the named risks.
3. ADDRESSED. `tests/e2e/inbox.spec.ts:689-748` seeds 22 notices in 11 shared-`created_at` pairs. The 20-row page ends mid-pair. It clicks "Show more" and asserts no duplicates, a total equal to the DB count (23), and that all 22 seeded rows appear. Rows are matched on distinct amounts, so a gap would fail. I did not run it; the implementer reports `pnpm test:e2e inbox` at 5 passed.
4. ADDRESSED (preferred option). The migration now has a per-row `begin ... exception when others` block and a `job_runs` row. I compared it with `generate_due_occurrences` and the pattern is identical (`status`, `details` with `inserted`, `failed`, `errors`, 20 errors max, message cut to 200). The columns match the table in `20261029130001_ledger.sql`: `job`, `finished_at`, `status` in ('running','ok','error'), `details`. The pgTAP assertion expects 11 ok rows, which matches the 11 `send_handover_reminders` calls in the test. `started_at = now()` scopes it to the test's own transaction.
5. ADDRESSED. `docs/fr-audit.md:38` and `:41` no longer say "pending until db:push". They say the migration and job are created when the migration is applied on merge, and the counts are gone. This matches `docs/deploy.md:~80`.
6. ADDRESSED. `docs/fr-audit.md:59` now says "Partly"; the tally at `:66` and `docs/roadmap.md:88` read nine met, two partly.
7. ADDRESSED. `links.ts` uses `notice.childName || "Your child"` and the detail reads `Due ${plural(days,"day")} ago`. Both are covered by new or updated tests in `links.test.ts`.
8. ADDRESSED. `notifications.test.sql` counts are scoped by `family_id`. `inbox_reminders.test.sql` asserts family-scoped cumulative counts (3, 3, 3, 3, 5, 5, 7, 7, 7, 9, 11). These still check the real behaviour: due day, rerun dedupe, quiet days, day-3 and day-6 marks, confirmed occurrence, late first sight and paused schedule. A test that asserted only the global return value would be weaker, so nothing is lost. A few unscoped assertions remain (`kind = 'allowance_due' and recipient_id in (...)` and similar), but they are keyed to the test's own users, so they are safe.
9. ADDRESSED. The test now inserts a pending `parent_gifts` row with `give_on` equal to the due day and asserts no notice has its id as `source_id`. The assertion is now real.

**New breakage (Critical/Important)**

None found.

- **Prerender and Cache Components:** `InboxRefresh` sits in `<Suspense>`, so `usePathname` outside a boundary is not a problem. The `(app)` routes are not dynamic-param routes.
- **Loops:** none. `router.refresh()` changes neither `pathname` nor visibility, so it cannot re-trigger either effect. `previous.current` is updated after every pathname change.
- **Offline:** respected. Both paths check `navigator.onLine !== false`.
- **Migration edit in place:** safe, because the migration is not on `main` and so not applied to production. `exception when others` does not catch query-cancel, which is correct. Errors are recorded rather than swallowed: `sqlstate`, message, `occurrence_id` and `status='error'` all go to `job_runs`. This is the same as the existing generator, with the same known gap (a failure outside the loop rolls back the run row).

**Minor (deferred)**

- `InboxRefresh` lives in the `(app)` layout, so it also fires `router.refresh()` on every tab refocus on pages with no inbox badge (`/invite`, `/settings`, `/dashboard`). The cost is a wasted RSC refetch, with no correctness impact. Mounting it in the child and parent shells would be tighter.
- On client navigation into the inbox, the page renders once and then `refresh()` re-renders it. Harmless.
- Nothing tests the `exception` branch of `send_handover_reminders` (no pgTAP asserting an `error` row with `details.errors`).
- A `job_runs` status of `error` has no alert or surfacing, as with the generator.
- The e2e test finds the user via `auth.admin.listUsers({perPage: 1000})`, which is fine locally. It would miss the user if the local project ever held more than 1000 users.
- The implementer's note about `pnpm db:start` printing local demo keys is not a hosted credential. No action needed.

Verdict: all findings addressed
