### Spec Compliance

**Verdict: Approved.** The migration matches the brief. I found no spec gaps and no Critical or Important issues. I read the diff and the original `held_spend` in the purchases migration, and did not re-run the pgTAP suite.

- ✅ `requests` table, `20261030185541_requests.sql:5-26`
  - Columns match the brief: `kind` and `status` check constraints use exactly the brief's values, and `amount_minor` is bigint with a 1..100000000000 check.
  - `reason` and `decision_note` are limited to 140 characters.
  - `auto` defaults to false, `occurred_at` is `not null default now()`, and `unique (child_id, idempotency_key)` is present.
  - Both lifecycle checks are present: `requests_withdrawal_never_confirmed` and `requests_deposit_never_approved`.
  - The `(child_id, status)` index exists. Extra FK and `family_id` indexes and an `updated_at` trigger are harmless additions.
- ✅ RLS, `:42-50`. RLS is enabled, and there is one select policy for `is_self or is_parent_of`, wrapped in `(select …)`.
- ✅ `held_spend` replacement, `:79-99`.
  - Signature `(uuid) returns bigint`, `stable`, `security definer`, `search_path = ''`, and the same `JA403` guard as the Task 3 version.
  - `create or replace` keeps the existing ACL, so the grants made in `20261029183422_purchases.sql` (around line 365) are preserved.
  - `record_purchase` calls `held_spend` at `purchases.sql:178` and relies on the same signature, so it keeps working.
  - It now sums pending deposits only, as the brief says.
- ✅ `reserved_save`, `available_save` and `available_spend`.
  - All four are authorized to self or parent only, and raise `JA403` otherwise.
  - Each takes `p_child_id` and checks it against the caller, so it cannot read another family's balances. The test covers a sibling and the other family for all four functions (diff lines 326-332).
  - Revoke from `public, anon` and grant to `authenticated`, as the constraints require.
- ✅ `request_window_total`.
  - The window is `p_window_start is null or occurred_at >= p_window_start`, so null means all time and the bound is inclusive.
  - It is internal with no grants. It revokes from `public, anon, authenticated`, and the test confirms `authenticated` gets `42501`.
- ✅ Tests, `requests.test.sql`.
  - The `plan(23)` count matches the assertions: 5 + 5 + 2 + 2 + 5 + 2 + 2.
  - Coverage includes all brief cases, plus a window total that excludes the older row and the all-time total.
- ✅ Docs and types. `docs/database.md` documents the table and all five functions, and `held_spend` no longer says "returns 0 until slice 4". The brief also required regenerated types, and the diff lists `src/types/database.types.ts` with 50 added lines. I did not inspect those lines.
- ⚠️ Rule 7, "explicit per-operation policies" versus the brief's "No write policies". The diff satisfies the intent.
  - It revokes `insert, update, delete, truncate` from `authenticated` and all privileges from `anon` (`:44-45`), so denial is enforced by privilege rather than by an absent policy.
  - The pgTAP insert test asserts `42501`.
  - The existing tables (`ledger_entries`, `money_rules` and others) follow the same pattern, so this is the established repo convention. I'm flagging the wording conflict between CLAUDE.md and the brief as plan-mandated, not as a defect.

### Strengths
- The helper functions reuse one authorization guard, and the nested calls (`available_*` calling `balance`, `reserved_save` and `held_spend`) stay safe under definer rights.
- The `occurred_at` window uses an inclusive bound that matches the "single counter" design.
- Table privileges are revoked, not just policies omitted, which gives defense in depth.
- The tests check negative paths: sibling, other family, a check violation per rule, and the unique key.

### Issues

**Critical:** none.

**Important:** none.

**Minor**
- `requests.sql:7-8`: nothing ties `family_id` to the child's family at the DB level. This is plan-mandated, and the Task 9/10 definer functions derive it, so it is acceptable. Other tables handle it the same way.
- `requests.sql:14-15`: `reason` and `decision_note` have a length check but no trim or empty-to-null check. The constraints say the writing functions do the trimming, so this belongs to later tasks.
- `requests.test.sql`: there is no assertion that a sibling parent cannot call `available_*`, but that path is the same guard as `reserved_save`. There is also no negative case for `available_save` when reserved exceeds the balance. Optional.

### Assessment
Task quality: **Approved**

## Controller ruling (binding)
Notice payloads: `withdrawal_requested` → `{"amount_minor": <amount>, "reason": <reason or null>}`; `withdrawal_decided` → `{"amount_minor": <amount>, "approved": <boolean>}`. The merged inbox copy reads exactly these keys. Use `source_type 'request'`, `source_id` = request id, `window_key` null.

timestamp: 2026-10-30T19:06:37.819Z

chars: 7557

**Verdict: Approved.** Nothing Critical or Important. Three Minor items below; none blocks the task.

### Spec Compliance
- ✅ create_withdrawal is child only. It checks `is_self` plus an approved child membership, and both failures raise JA403 (migration 20261030190114_request_functions.sql:77-88).
- ✅ Validation in create_withdrawal (:90-95): amount must be 1..1e11, the reason is trimmed with empty becoming null and a 140 limit, and the key must be non-blank and at most 200. Anything else raises 22023.
- ✅ create_withdrawal takes the advisory lock only, before the idempotency lookup (:98-109). This matches the lock-order rule for create functions with no source row.
- ✅ A retried key returns the current status and id (:101-109). A key that belongs to a deposit raises JA409.
- ✅ `over_available` returns `available_minor` (:111-114).
- ✅ Caps (:117-135). Rules are checked in item, week, month order and the first broken one is returned. Windows use `rule_window_start(family_id, period, now())`, so they follow the family timezone. Statuses counted are pending, approved and handed_over. The item rule checks `amount > cap`.
- ✅ Auto-approve (:138-157) needs at least one rule and every rule must pass. Statuses counted are approved and handed_over. It sets `auto = true` and sends no notice (:159-172).
- ✅ The `withdrawal_requested` notice matches the controller ruling: payload `{amount_minor, reason}`, source `request`, window_key null (:168-172). The test asserts this at withdrawals.test.sql:450-455.
- ✅ decide_withdrawal (:201-233) locks the row first, then the advisory lock. A status other than pending returns `already_decided`. Approve re-checks `amount <= balance − reserved + amount` and returns `over_available` if that fails. It sets decided_by, decided_at and the trimmed note, and the `withdrawal_decided` notice carries `{amount_minor, approved}`.
- ✅ complete_request (:307-326) locks the row, then the advisory lock. `handed_over` returns `already_completed`; any status other than approved, or a kind other than withdrawal, returns `not_approved`. It then calls post_request.
- ✅ post_request (:263-282) re-checks only Save, using `balance − (reserved − amount)`. It posts `withdrawal_out` (save) and `withdrawal_in` (spend) with keys `request:<id>:out` / `:in`, `source_type request`, and the label is the reason or "Taken out of Save". It sets handed_over, completed_by and completed_at.
- ✅ post_request has no grants. It is revoked from public, anon and authenticated (:376), the same pattern as rule_window_start.
- ✅ cancel_request (:348-365) locks the row and posts nothing. The child can cancel only their own pending request; a parent can cancel a pending or approved withdrawal. Returns `cancelled` or `already_closed`.
- ✅ The functions callable by users are revoked from public and anon and granted to authenticated (:378-390). All of them are `security definer set search_path = ''`, and none takes a time from the caller.
- ✅ Every test scenario in the brief is covered. The test file has 66 assertions, matching `plan(66)`. Extra coverage includes the balance drop before approve/complete (C7) and the notice source and window key.
- ⚠️ I could not verify Review Focus 3 (five parallel completes, two parallel approvals) by running anything. The plan defers it to Task 14, which is end-to-end. The reasoning below says it holds.

### Named risks, traced
- **Exactly-once complete:** holds. `select ... for update` under READ COMMITTED gives a waiting transaction the latest row version, so the second caller sees `handed_over` and returns `already_completed`. The unique constraint on `ledger_entries.idempotency_key` (20261029130001_ledger.sql:44) together with the derived `request:<id>:out|in` keys is a hard backstop.
- **Parallel approvals overdrawing Save:** holds. The advisory lock serialises approvals for the same child. The functions are volatile plpgsql, so each statement after the lock takes a fresh snapshot, and `balance` / `reserved_save` see the other approval once it commits. Both pending requests are already counted in `reserved_save`, so the check reduces to `balance >= total reserved`, which is the intended formula.
- **Idempotent create under a race:** holds. The existence lookup runs under the per-child advisory lock, so a second call with the same key sees the committed first request. The unique `(child_id, idempotency_key)` constraint (requests.sql:22) cannot fire on that path, so the missing `unique_violation` handler is acceptable.
- **Windows:** correct. The lower bound comes from `rule_window_start` in the family timezone, and `occurred_at` defaults to `now()` (requests.sql:18), so no request can be dated in the future. Status sets match the brief.
- **Completing a cancelled or rejected request:** impossible. Only `approved` reaches post_request (:322). Cancel and complete both lock the row, so they serialise. The test at :524 covers a rejected request.
- **Child, or another family's parent, deciding or completing:** blocked. Both go through `is_parent_of`, which requires an approved parent and child in the same family. Tests at :480-485 cover it.
- **The unused complete_request key:** retries are still safe without it. Safety comes from the row lock, the status check and the unique ledger keys; the key adds nothing beyond input validation. This is documented at :311 and in docs/database.md, and matches the existing `confirm_gift_handover` convention.

### Strengths
- The code follows the lock order exactly.
- A missing request and a foreign request both raise JA403, so a request's existence does not leak.
- Posting is isolated in one internal function, as the brief asked.
- Tests assert that the notice payload, source and window key match the controller ruling.
- Review Focus 5 (approved request still hands over after the cap is lowered) and both Save re-checks are tested.
- docs/database.md is updated and accurate.

### Issues
**Critical:** none.

**Important:** none.

**Minor:**
1. **post_request relies on the caller's JWT** (migration :263-264). It calls `public.balance` and `public.reserved_save`, which raise JA403 unless `auth.uid()` is the child or their parent (requests.sql:63, ledger.sql:98). That is fine for complete_request now. A future "provider seam" caller without a user session (cron, webhook, service role) would get JA403. Fix when that caller arrives: read the sums directly, or add internal unchecked helpers.
2. **post_request returns `completed` for any kind other than withdrawal** without posting anything (:260, :285). The path is unreachable today because complete_request filters kind first. Task 10 extends this function, though, so a missed branch would report success silently. Fix: raise 22023 in an `else` branch, or have Task 10 add an explicit deposit branch.
3. **Small reporting quirks:**
   - The item over_cap returns `used_minor 0` (:124). The brief does not define this case and 0 is defensible, but the UI should not render it as "used 0".
   - A child cancelling an approved request gets `already_closed` (:365) even though the request is still open. The brief allows only two return values, so this is acceptable; the app should word the message accordingly.

### Assessment
**Task quality: Approved.**
- **Spec:** everything in the brief, the constraints and the controller ruling is met.
- **Concurrency:** sound on all named risks, though only by reasoning (no race test until Task 14).
- **Hand-off to Task 10:** Minor items 1 and 2 should be handled when Task 10 extends post_request.

**Verdict: Approved.** Task 10 meets the brief and the binding constraints. I found no Critical or Important issues, only three Minor ones. I did not re-run the test suites.

### Spec Compliance
- ✅ `create_deposit` is child only: `is_self` check plus an approved child membership, otherwise `JA403` (migration `20261030190114_request_functions.sql` diff ~L164-175).
- ✅ Input checks raise `22023` for amount outside 1..1e11, a blank key, or a key over 200.
- ✅ It takes the advisory lock only (no source row), then handles the idempotent retry. A key already used by a withdrawal raises `JA409` (~L183-193).
- ✅ `amount > available_spend` returns `{status: over_available, available_minor}` and stores nothing (~L195-198; test L395-396).
- ✅ Auto-confirm needs at least one rule and every rule must pass. Week and month rules count `request_window_total(deposit, {confirmed})`; item rules use `used = 0` (~L201-216). The window start uses `now()`; no caller-supplied time.
- ✅ The auto path inserts the request as `pending`, calls `post_request` in the same transaction under the same lock, sets `auto = true`, and sends no notice (~L218-226; test L497 asserts zero notifications).
- ✅ The non-auto path calls `notify_parents('deposit_requested', {amount_minor})` with source `request` and window key null (test L377-382).
- ✅ `post_request` now has an explicit deposit branch: `deposit_out` (spend) and `deposit_in` (save), label `'Put into Save'`, keys `request:<id>:out` / `request:<id>:in`, sets `confirmed` with `completed_by/at`. An unknown kind raises `22023` (diff L67-88). Still no grants (L301).
- ✅ The confirm re-check is `amount > balance(spend) − (held_spend − amount)`, which is right because this request is still pending and inside `held_spend` when checked. Test L478-485 shows a refused confirm stays `pending`.
- ✅ `complete_request`: `confirmed` returns `already_completed`. Only an approved withdrawal or a pending deposit proceeds; anything else returns `not_approved` (diff L116-127).
- ✅ Task 9's withdrawal path is not regressed. `handed_over` still returns `already_completed`, and the table constraint `requests_withdrawal_never_confirmed` makes the new `confirmed` check unreachable for withdrawals.
- ✅ `decide_deposit` is parent only; a missing request is also `JA403`. A non-deposit or a note over 140 raises `22023`. It takes the row lock `for update`, then the ledger lock, and anything not `pending` returns `already_decided`. The note is trimmed and empty becomes null. It notifies the child with `deposit_decided {amount_minor, approved: false}` and posts nothing (~L244-279; tests L451-472).
- ✅ Review Focus 4 is tested on the second child (C2). With overspend off the purchase returns `not_enough_spend` with `free 0`; with overspend on it is still `not_enough_spend`; the deposit then confirms as `completed` (test L419-429). This matches `record_purchase`'s held check (`purchases.sql:186-187`).
- ✅ Grants: both new functions are revoked from `public` and `anon` and granted to `authenticated` only. The test covers `anon` → `42501` and `post_request` not granted → `42501`.
- ✅ `docs/database.md` is updated for all four functions.

### Named-risk checks
- **Double post or notice on auto-confirm retry:** not possible. A retry returns early at the existing-request lookup, before any rule evaluation or posting. The ledger keys derive from the request id, so a second post would also hit the unique key. `post_request` has no `notify` call (`notify` appears only at L126, 186, 408, 454).
- **Auto-confirm cannot be refused by `post_request`:** the pre-insert check (`amount ≤ balance − held_before`) is algebraically the same as the post-insert re-check (`balance − (held_after − amount)`). Every path that lowers Spend (purchases, deposits) takes the same ledger lock.
- **Child cancel vs a parent's concurrent confirm:** both take the request row `for update`. Under READ COMMITTED, whichever goes second re-reads the latest row. Cancel then confirm gives `not_approved`. Confirm then cancel gives `already_closed`.
- **Actor consistency:** `ledger_entries.created_by` records the user who caused the action. `record_purchase` uses the child (`purchases.sql:196`) and handovers use the parent, so the child as `created_by` on an auto-confirmed deposit is consistent. One asymmetry: auto-approved withdrawals leave `decided_by` null, while auto-confirmed deposits set `completed_by` to the child. This is defensible, because a deposit has no human completion step and `auto = true` tells them apart.

### Strengths
- `post_request` is still the only path that posts a request to the ledger, with a fail-closed `22023` for unknown kinds.
- The hold arithmetic is exact and matches the purchase-side held check.
- `JA403` for a missing request does not reveal whether the request exists.
- The 54 assertions cover every case in the brief, plus the Spend re-check at confirm, the cross-kind `JA409`, and an outsider parent.

### Issues
**Critical:** none. **Important:** none.

**Minor**
1. `create_deposit` (diff ~L224) ignores the result of `perform public.post_request(...)`. If a future change made posting refusable, the function would return `confirmed` with `auto = true` while the row stays `pending`. Fix: read the result into a variable and raise if it is not `'completed'`. This is cheap hardening, since the invariant holds today.
2. A few test gaps, none plan-mandated:
   - no double-tap test on an auto-confirmed deposit (should return `confirmed`, still 2 rows);
   - no child-cancel-of-a-pending-deposit test showing the hold is released;
   - no assertion that the refused overspend-on purchase (test L426) posted no ledger row;
   - no month-window auto-confirm test.
3. `completed_by` holds the child for auto-confirms (see the actor point above). The docs mention it, but a future UI reading `completed_by` as "confirmed by" will need to check `auto` first. Consider a column comment on `requests.completed_by`.

### Assessment
Task quality: **Approved**. None of the Minor items block this task.

Files reviewed:

**Verdict: Approved.** Nothing blocks this task. Two Important items should go on a follow-up list.

### Spec Compliance
- ✅ Types match the brief (`RequestKind`, the six statuses, `SavingsRequest`, `Availability` with `takeOutLimitMinor`, camelCase result unions). `src/features/requests/types.ts:1-51`
- ✅ `statusLabel` covers every kind × status pair. `overCapMessage` reproduces the spec example exactly and drops the second sentence when `used` is 0, so "You can take out up to £15 a week." reads naturally (the controller's note). `describe.ts:289-319`
- ✅ Schemas: reason and note are trimmed, empty becomes undefined, max 140. Amount 0 is rejected, idempotency keys are uuids, and `completeSchema` needs both ids. `schemas.ts`
- ✅ Readers: `getAvailability` and `listRequests` use `assertSelfOrParentOf`. `listRequestsNeedingParent` uses `assertParentOf`, and its `.or` filter is valid PostgREST. `service.ts:1018-1057`, `repository.ts:659-671`
- ✅ Mutations: child creates go through `assertRole("child")` and use `user.id` (no child id from the client). Parent mutations go through `childOf` (RLS select) and then `assertParentOf`. Cancel uses `assertSelfOrParentOf`. `service.ts:1060-1128`
- ✅ Completing returns `already_completed` as success. `not_approved` and `over_available` at completion throw `AppError("CONFLICT", "This request changed. Refresh to see where it is.")`. `service.ts:1112-1122`
- ✅ There is one `createAction` per mutation and each calls `refresh()`. The async currency schema factory is supported (`src/lib/action.ts:46`). Exporting actions from the client barrel matches diary, rules and allowance. `server/actions.ts`, `index.ts`
- ✅ Ledger history adds `withdrawal` and `deposit`, one line per request. `ledger/server/service.ts:144-173`
- ✅ `routes.childRequests` added. `src/config/site.ts:36-37`
- ✅ All required tests are present, including `takeOutLimitMinor` as min(available Save, each cap − used).

### Named risks, checked
1. **Copy of the window logic in TS.** SQL is `date_trunc('week'|'month', p_at at time zone tz) at time zone tz`, which gives an ISO Monday week and a calendar month (`20261029182402_money_rules.sql:176-190`). `window.ts` matches this:
   - It takes the local date with `Intl`, steps back `(weekday+6)%7` days, and converts local midnight to UTC with a two-pass offset.
   - The Kolkata Monday 00:30 case and the London BST month case are tested.
   - The counted statuses (`pending, approved, handed_over`), the `occurred_at` column and the `item` → 0 rule match `create_withdrawal` (`request_functions.sql:72-93`) and `request_window_total`.
   - Drift is possible only in zones where local midnight does not exist (DST jumping at 00:00, e.g. Cuba or Chile). Even there Monday and the 1st are rarely transition days.
   - Severity: display only, since SQL still enforces the cap. A mismatch shows a wrong "You can take out up to" figure and then an `over_cap` refusal. Acceptable for this task (Important, below).
2. **Authorization: no hole found.**
   - A child calling a parent mutation: RLS returns their own child id, then `assertParentOf` throws.
   - Another family's parent: RLS hides the row, so it is FORBIDDEN before any RPC runs.
   - SQL re-checks everything with JA403.
   - `getChildRules` is self-or-parent, so a child can call `getAvailability`.
3. **Ledger grouping: correct.**
   - `groupBySource` only takes `occurrence` and `parent_gift` sources, and the purchase filter is `kind === "purchase"`, so request rows can't be double-counted or pollute those lines.
   - The out/in pair has the same amount and the same `occurred_at` (single insert, `now()`). The strict `> cutoff` page filter therefore drops a pair split across the 50-row page boundary and keeps it for the next page.
   - Undone and purchase lines are unaffected.
4. **Result mapping.** Decide-withdrawal `over_available` comes back as `{ ok: true, data: "over_available" }`. The UI can show it only if it inspects `data`; it must not treat `ok` as approved. That is for the UI task (Minor, below).
5. **Idempotency keys** go from the schemas through the service to `p_idempotency_key` for create_withdrawal, create_deposit and complete_request. Decide, reject and cancel need no key because they are idempotent by status in SQL. ✅

### Strengths
- The repository validates every RPC result and throws INTERNAL on any unknown shape. Only the repository touches table, column and function names.
- `completeRequest`'s return type narrows the result, so callers can't see a conflict status.
- Thorough window tests (non-UTC zone, DST).

### Issues
**Critical:** none.

**Important (deferrable):**
- `src/features/requests/window.ts` with `repository.ts:605-620`: the cap window maths is a second source of truth next to SQL `rule_window_start` and `request_window_total`. It costs one PostgREST query per period cap on top of the 6 balance RPCs.
  - Fix (follow-up migration task): add a granted, self-or-parent `security definer` function such as `withdrawal_cap_remaining(p_child_id)` returning the remaining amount per period, built on `rule_window_start` and `request_window_total`. Then delete `window.ts` and `sumWithdrawalsSince`.
  - Not blocking: display only, matches on every realistic zone, and the report flags it.
- `schemas.ts:436`: `approve: z.preprocess(v => v === true || v === "true", z.boolean())` turns a missing or malformed `approve` into `false`. A buggy form would silently reject the request and notify the child "Not approved".
  - Fix: `z.union([z.boolean(), z.enum(["true","false"]).transform(v => v === "true")])` so anything else fails validation.

**Minor:**
- `ledger/server/service.ts:164`: the "Take out" / "Put in" fallback never runs, because SQL always sets the label (`coalesce(reason, 'Taken out of Save')`, deposits "Put into Save"; `request_functions.sql:225`). The ledger test uses `label: null`, a state that can't happen. Either drop the fallback or use the real labels in the test.
- `ledger/components/history-list.tsx:46-53`: withdrawal and deposit lines render as a plain amount, the same as income. Jar transfers are not income (hard rule 14), so the history UI task should give them a transfer treatment. Out of scope here; flag for the UI task.
- `describe.ts:305`: the item period reads "each time" while the spec chip copy is "per time". Fine in a sentence, but it deviates from the verbatim copy list. Confirm with the spec owner.
- `server/actions.ts:495`: `rejectDepositAction` can't pass a note, though the service and SQL accept one. Harmless unless the UI wants a note on "Didn't get it".
- `service.ts:1111`: the docstring says "Hands an approved withdrawal over", but the function also confirms pending deposits. Update the comment.

### Assessment
Task quality: **Approved.** Open a follow-up for the SQL `take_out_limit` function, and tighten the `approve` parsing before the parent UI wires it.

  - null gives null, meaning no cap. The UI limit is then not bounded by availableSave, which is the same as the old behaviour, since the old code also returned null with no rules.
  - 0 gives min(avail, 0) = 0 and blocks the request, as intended.
  - `fetchCapRemaining` maps null and undefined to null and wraps the number, so a bigint returned as a string is handled.

Minor (deferred)
- No test shows the note reaching `callDecideDeposit` through `rejectDeposit` or the action. The existing `rejectDeposit` call in the service test has no note assertion. Only the schema is tested.
- `label ?? ""` in the ledger service would show a blank line if a request row ever came back unlabelled. It is unreachable per the SQL.
- `getAvailability` now makes one rpc call instead of reading rules and family. This is fine.

Verdict: all findings addressed

**Verdict: Needs fixes.** Every child flow and every parent button is there. One real behaviour bug blocks: the parent UI ignores the result data from `completeRequest`, `cancelRequest` and `rejectDeposit`, so it can tell a parent something happened when it did not.

### Spec Compliance
- ✅ Take-out sheet shows "You can take out up to <amount>", the amount field, "What's it for?" and "Ask". The four results map to the spec strings (take-out-sheet.tsx:1269-1280). The limit is `takeOutLimitMinor ?? availableSaveMinor` (request-actions.tsx:931). This follows ruling 2. The type doc says the cap is already no more than free Save, so the spec's min() holds.
- ✅ Put-in sheet shows "Spend has <amount>", "Put in", "Waiting for your parent to confirm" and "Added to Save" (put-in-sheet.tsx:818, 833-842).
- ✅ Child rule lines in both sheets come from `describeRuleForChild` (request-actions.tsx:926-929).
- ✅ Request list: status chips use the verbatim words Waiting / Approved / Done / Not approved / Cancelled, with no red (rejected and cancelled map to "closed"). Cancel shows on pending rows only (request-list.tsx:1120). On `already_closed` it shows "This one already moved on." and refreshes, which is ruling 4 (request-list.tsx:1097-1099).
- ✅ Parent items: the three sentences and the buttons Approve / Not now, Confirm / "Didn't hand it over" (with a confirm step) and Confirm / Didn't get it match the brief (parent-request-items.tsx:652-688).
- ✅ Decide result: over_available gives calm copy, and already_decided falls through to a refresh (parent-request-items.tsx:617-629). This is ruling 3.
- ❌ The complete, cancel and reject-deposit result data is never read (parent-request-items.tsx:631-650). See Critical 1.
- ✅ `runOnline` wraps every mutation: take-out :1294, put-in :854, request-list :1093, and the parent `run()` helper :608.
- ✅ Idempotency keys:
  - Take-out and put-in use `useState(newId)` inside the form. The form sits in the portalled sheet popup, so it unmounts on close and the next open gets a new key. A retry or double tap reuses the key.
  - Reusing the key after a refusal is safe. I checked request_functions.sql:60-70: over_cap and over_available return before any insert, so no row is stored against the key.
  - The parent Confirm key is a lazy per-row ref. That is safe because each row is one request.
- ✅ Cache Components:
  - Child Home and /child/requests do all reads inside `<Suspense>`.
  - Parent `RequestsSection` and child-detail `ChildRequests` are each in their own `<Suspense>`.
  - No layout was touched.
- ✅ Right child only: `listRequestsNeedingParent` calls `assertParentOf(childId)` and then reads by childId (service.ts:61-64). The nickname comes from the same child record.
- ✅ Purchase-sheet claim is correct.
  - `record_purchase` (redefined in 20261030130002) delegates to `record_purchase_at`. That function computes `v_balance - v_held` from `held_spend` (purchases.sql:178-188), and requests.sql:79 redefines `held_spend` to count real pending deposits.
  - The sheet shows `freeMinor` through `notEnoughSpendMessage`, which uses the verbatim copy.
  - Not changing the sheet is acceptable.
- ✅ Rules form:
  - Has the three section titles, a `summarise` line in each, and "Deposits will count before you get the cash".
  - Row copy comes from `describeRule`, verbatim (describe.ts:16-21).
- ⚠️ Rules form group legends "Most they can take out", "Without asking you" and "Without you confirming" are not in the spec copy list (rules-form.tsx:1519, 1527, 1541). These are the invented titles you flagged.
- ✅ History shows "Save → Spend" and "Spend → Save" with no sign, and a test covers it (history-list.tsx:369-372, test :338-354). This is ruling 6.
- ✅ Child Home edits are minimal, which is ruling 5.

### Strengths
- Business refusals stay in the form with clear copy. Only pending or approved results refresh the page.
- The re-wrap of `start()` after the await is handled correctly.
- The "Didn't hand it over" confirm dialog explains the outcome in plain words.
- The parent Confirm key is made lazily, which avoids a hydration mismatch.
- The double-Confirm same-key test asserts real behaviour.

### Issues

**Critical**
1. **False success copy on Confirm, Cancel and Didn't get it** (parent-request-items.tsx:631-650).
   - `complete()` always toasts "Handed over." or "Added to Save.". But `CompleteResult` is `"completed" | "already_completed" | "not_approved" | "over_available"` (types.ts).
   - `over_available` is a planned path. Review Focus 5 says Save is re-checked at handover, and post_request returns over_available when Save no longer covers it. In that case the parent is told "Handed over." while nothing posted and the row stays.
   - `cancel()` toasts "Okay, left as it was." even when the result is `already_closed`.
   - `rejectDeposit()` toasts "Okay, not added." even on `already_decided`, for example when the other parent already confirmed it.
   - Fix: switch on `data` in each case. `completed` gets the success toast. `already_completed`, `already_closed` and `already_decided` get a calm "Already sorted." plus a refresh. `over_available` sets an error like "There isn't enough in Save for this now." (or "...in Spend" for a deposit). `not_approved` gets a refresh.
   - Add tests for `completeRequestAction` returning over_available and `rejectDepositAction` returning already_decided.

**Important**
2. **Child Cancel is below the 44 px target** (request-list.tsx:1121-1124). It uses `size="sm"`, which is `h-9`, so 36 px, on a child screen. This breaks design §11, which is plan-mandated. Fix: use the default size, or add `min-h-11`.
3. **Request dates can mismatch or show the wrong day** (request-list.tsx:1057). The module-level `Intl.DateTimeFormat` has no `timeZone`. A client component's SSR runs in the server timezone (UTC) and hydrates in the device timezone, so near midnight you get a hydration text mismatch, and the day may differ from the family's day. Fix: format on the server with the family timezone and pass the string down, or set `timeZone` explicitly.
4. **Copy outside the verbatim list** (rules-form.tsx:1519, 1527, 1541):
   - The three rules-form legends.
   - Parent toasts "Approved. Hand <amount> to <nickname>.", "Not now. We let them know.", "Okay, left as it was." and "Okay, not added.".
   - The child-home link "See your Save requests" and the sheet titles "Take money out of Save" and "Put money into Save".
   - The legends are the most visible because they label the spec's rows. Either get them signed off or drop them and let the verbatim row sentences carry the meaning.

**Minor**
5. parent/page.tsx:283 and :288 both use `rise(1)`, so the Requests and Children sections animate together. Bump Children to `rise(2)`.
6. Test gaps, since tests were written after the code:
   - No test that a new sheet open gets a new idempotency key.
   - No put-in over_available test.
   - No already_decided refresh test.
   - The take-out over_available test leans on `getAllByText(...).length > 0`, though the `role=status` assertion saves it.
   - The other tests do assert behaviour: the action arguments, refresh or no refresh, the same key on retry, the confirm-before-cancel step, and the no-sign history. They would fail on real regressions in the paths they cover.
7. The parent `decide` refreshes after over_available, which is harmless but not needed.

### Assessment
**Task quality: Needs fixes.** Critical 1 is required: read the result data for complete, cancel and reject-deposit, and add tests. Important 2 is the plan-mandated 44 px rule. Items 3 and 4 should be fixed or signed off. Everything else matches the brief and rulings 1–6.

**Spec Compliance**
- ✅ Scenario 1, take-out 500 (requests.spec.ts:145-164). It asserts "Sent to your parent" and the parent's "Sam wants US$500.00". It approves, then confirms "Hand over US$500.00 to Sam". The child sees Save 1,500 and Spend 1,500, and the "Save → Spend" history line. The parent view also shows 1,500/1,500.
- ✅ Scenario 2, auto-approve per time 300 (:166-174). It asserts the exact "Approved, your parent will hand it over" and the parent's "Hand over US$200.00 to Sam". The rule row index is correct: the take-out section has cap item, week and month, then auto-approve item at index 3.
- ✅ Scenario 3, put-in 300 (:176-188). It asserts "Waiting for your parent to confirm", then the parent's "Sam gave you US$300.00?" and Confirm. Save goes to 1,800, which also confirms the earlier approved-but-unhanded 200 does not move Save.
- ✅ Scenario 4, auto-confirm a week 500 (:190-196). It asserts "Added to Save" and Save at 2,000. Put-in index 1 is the week row, which is correct.
- ✅ Scenario 5, over-limit (:198-203). It asserts the `/^You can take out up to US\$/` copy.
- ✅ The project is registered in playwright.config.ts:51. The spec is serial, uses two contexts, and seeds Save 2000 and Spend 1000 through an allowance handover in the first test.
- ❌ The purchases.spec.ts scope fix is not in commit b10cb8e. `git show --stat` lists only the log, playwright.config.ts and requests.spec.ts, and `git status` still shows ` M tests/e2e/purchases.spec.ts`. The commit body claims the fix. The committed purchases spec still has the strict-mode failure on the "A week" switch, so the branch is broken at that commit.

**Issues**

Critical
- None.

Important
1. **tests/e2e/purchases.spec.ts (uncommitted).**
   - What: the scoping change is only in the working tree, and the commit body says it is included.
   - Why: the branch at HEAD fails purchases e2e.
   - Fix: commit it, either with `git commit --amend` or as `test(e2e): scope purchases week limit to Spending`.
   - Intent check: the change keeps the original intent. It scopes the switch, the label and Save to `section[aria-labelledby=spending-heading]`, and it is the right fix for the duplicate names.
2. **Accessibility defect (Task 12 code): src/features/rules/components/rule-row.tsx:93-96 and :104.**
   - What: `RuleRow` labels every switch and amount field only by period.
     - The label text is `PERIOD_LABEL[period]`: "Per time", "A week", "A month".
     - The amount field is labelled `${PERIOD_LABEL[period]} limit`.
     - `RuleGroup` and the Spending list in rules-form.tsx reuse these across sections, so "A week" appears 4 times (spend limit, cap, auto-approve is "Per time" only, deposit).
     - "Per time" appears 3 times: spend limit, cap and auto-approve.
     - The "A week limit" field is duplicated 3 times.
   - Why: a screen-reader user tabbing through controls hears the same names with no context. This violates the design.md §11 requirement that input names be distinguishable. It also breaks WCAG 1.3.1 and 2.4.6, with some risk under 4.1.2, because the section heading is not associated with the control. It is also what forced the index-based test locators.
   - Fix, preferably in a Task 12 follow-up:
     - (a) Wrap each section in `role="group"` with `aria-labelledby` pointing at its heading, and wrap each `RuleGroup` in a group labelled by a small kind heading such as "Most they can take out" / "Auto-approve". The accessible names stay, but screen readers announce the group context.
     - (b) Alternatively, add `aria-describedby` on the Switch pointing at the row's sentence `<p>`, which already reads "Tell me when Sam spends more than…". Give the sentence an id. This is cheap and preserves the existing test names.
     - (c) Or use `aria-labelledby="{id}-label {id}-sentence"` on the Switch and the amount field.
   - Test impact: with (a) or (b) the exact-name locators keep working, as long as tests stay section-scoped. With (c) the names change and tests need updating.
   - Grade: Important, not blocking Task 13. It is Task 12's defect and should be tracked as a follow-up.

Minor
1. **requests.spec.ts:74-80.** Index locators depend on DOM order and the row count in `RuleGroup`. Scoping by section is good and the comments document the order. Once the a11y fix gives rows distinct names or group roles, switch to `getByRole("group", { name })`. Add `expect(row.getByRole("switch")).toBeChecked()` before filling to guard against a hydration race. The `fill` after the click can otherwise run before `on` flips, though Playwright auto-wait on the amount field covers this.
2. **requests.spec.ts:80.** `getByText("Saved").first()` could match a stale sonner toast. It is fine in practice since each `setRule` starts from a fresh `goto`, but asserting the row's sentence text would be sturdier.
3. **requests.spec.ts:190-196.** Scenario 4 relies on the earlier manually confirmed 300 either not counting toward the week auto-confirm window or counting inclusively (300+200=500). Verify this is the intended rule semantics. If the window sums confirmed deposits and the boundary is exclusive, the test is order-fragile. Consider a comment, or a cap of 1000.
4. **requests.spec.ts:201.** `getByRole("button", { name: "Close" })` can hit two elements (a sheet X button plus a Close button) in strict mode. It passed per the report, so this is low risk.
5. **requests.spec.ts:158-160, 187, 195.** Substring `toContainText("US$1,500")` also matches "US$1,500.00" and anything longer, such as "US$1,5000". This is acceptable here.

**Assessment: Needs fixes**

The spec itself is sound and covers all five scenarios with the required outcome assertions. Two things need action. The purchases.spec.ts fix must be committed, since the commit omitted it. The duplicate-accessible-name defect in Task 12's `RuleRow` should be filed and fixed as a follow-up.

**Spec Compliance**
- ✅ Scenario 1, take-out 500 (tests/e2e/requests.spec.ts:145-164). It asserts "Sent to your parent" and the parent's "Sam wants US$500.00". It approves, then confirms "Hand over US$500.00 to Sam". The child sees Save 1,500 and Spend 1,500, and the "Save → Spend" history line. The parent view also shows 1,500/1,500.
- ✅ Scenario 2, auto-approve per time 300 (:166-174). It asserts the exact "Approved, your parent will hand it over" and the parent's "Hand over US$200.00 to Sam". The rule row index is correct: the take-out section has cap item, week and month, then auto-approve item at index 3.
- ✅ Scenario 3, put-in 300 (:176-188). It asserts "Waiting for your parent to confirm", then the parent's "Sam gave you US$300.00?" and Confirm. Save goes to 1,800, which also confirms the earlier approved-but-unhanded 200 does not move Save.
- ✅ Scenario 4, auto-confirm a week 500 (:190-196). It asserts "Added to Save" and Save at 2,000. Put-in index 1 is the week row, which is correct.
- ✅ Scenario 5, over-limit (:198-203). It asserts the `/^You can take out up to US\$/` copy.
- ✅ The project is registered in playwright.config.ts:51. The spec is serial, uses two contexts, and seeds Save 2000 and Spend 1000 through an allowance handover in the first test.
- ❌ The purchases.spec.ts scope fix is not in commit b10cb8e. `git show --stat` lists only the log, playwright.config.ts and requests.spec.ts, and `git status` still shows ` M tests/e2e/purchases.spec.ts`. The commit body claims the fix. The committed purchases spec still has the strict-mode failure on the "A week" switch, so the branch is broken at that commit.

**Issues**

Critical
- None.

Important
1. **tests/e2e/purchases.spec.ts (uncommitted).**
   - What: the scoping change is only in the working tree, and the commit body says it is included.
   - Why: the branch at HEAD fails purchases e2e.
   - Fix: commit it, either with `git commit --amend` or as `test(e2e): scope purchases week limit to Spending`.
   - Intent check: the change keeps the original intent. It scopes the switch, the label and Save to `section[aria-labelledby=spending-heading]`, and it is the right fix for the duplicate names.
2. **Accessibility defect (Task 12 code): src/features/rules/components/rule-row.tsx:93-96 and :104.**
   - What: `RuleRow` labels every switch and amount field only by period.
     - The label text is `PERIOD_LABEL[period]`: "Per time", "A week", "A month".
     - The amount field is labelled `${PERIOD_LABEL[period]} limit`.
     - `RuleGroup` and the Spending list in rules-form.tsx reuse these across sections, so "A week" appears 4 times (spend limit, cap, auto-approve is "Per time" only, deposit).
     - "Per time" appears 3 times: spend limit, cap and auto-approve.
     - The "A week limit" field is duplicated 3 times.
   - Why: a screen-reader user tabbing through controls hears the same names with no context. This violates the design.md §11 requirement that input names be distinguishable. It also breaks WCAG 1.3.1 and 2.4.6, with some risk under 4.1.2, because the section heading is not associated with the control. It is also what forced the index-based test locators.
   - Fix, preferably in a Task 12 follow-up:
     - (a) Wrap each section in `role="group"` with `aria-labelledby` pointing at its heading, and wrap each `RuleGroup` in a group labelled by a small kind heading such as "Most they can take out" / "Auto-approve". The accessible names stay, but screen readers announce the group context.
     - (b) Alternatively, add `aria-describedby` on the Switch pointing at the row's sentence `<p>`, which already reads "Tell me when Sam spends more than…". Give the sentence an id. This is cheap and preserves the existing test names.
     - (c) Or use `aria-labelledby="{id}-label {id}-sentence"` on the Switch and the amount field.
   - Test impact: with (a) or (b) the exact-name locators keep working, as long as tests stay section-scoped. With (c) the names change and tests need updating.
   - Grade: Important, not blocking Task 13. It is Task 12's defect and should be tracked as a follow-up.

Minor
1. **requests.spec.ts:74-80.** Index locators depend on DOM order and the row count in `RuleGroup`. Scoping by section is good and the comments document the order. Once the a11y fix gives rows distinct names or group roles, switch to `getByRole("group", { name })`. Add `expect(row.getByRole("switch")).toBeChecked()` before filling to guard against a hydration race. The `fill` after the click can otherwise run before `on` flips, though Playwright auto-wait on the amount field covers this.
2. **requests.spec.ts:80.** `getByText("Saved").first()` could match a stale sonner toast. It is fine in practice since each `setRule` starts from a fresh `goto`, but asserting the row's sentence text would be sturdier.
3. **requests.spec.ts:190-196.** Scenario 4 relies on the earlier manually confirmed 300 either not counting toward the week auto-confirm window or counting inclusively (300+200=500). Verify this is the intended rule semantics. If the window sums confirmed deposits and the boundary is exclusive, the test is order-fragile. Consider a comment, or a cap of 1000.
4. **requests.spec.ts:201.** `getByRole("button", { name: "Close" })` can hit two elements (a sheet X button plus a Close button) in strict mode. It passed per the report, so this is low risk.
5. **requests.spec.ts:158-160, 187, 195.** Substring `toContainText("US$1,500")` also matches "US$1,500.00" and anything longer, such as "US$1,5000". This is acceptable here.

**Assessment: Needs fixes**

The spec itself is sound and covers all five scenarios with the required outcome assertions. Two things need action. The purchases.spec.ts fix must be committed, since the commit omitted it. The duplicate-accessible-name defect in Task 12's `RuleRow` should be filed and fixed as a follow-up.

The outcome is the same whichever call takes the lock first, because the hold and the spent balance both leave 0 free. That is correct, but the test cannot show an interleaving.
- ⚠️ Race 2: `:447-483`. The brief's literal scenario really is unreachable. `reserved_save` includes pending requests, so `create_withdrawal` refuses the second 600 against 1000. The two replacements are honest and assert exact statuses and counts.
- ✅ `assertParentCanRead` (`src/features/family/server/dal.ts:~119`). It requires the parent role and `isParentOf(child)` and skips only the profile-complete check. Its doc comment says it exists for reads. `assertSelfOrParentOf` already uses it, so this matches the existing pattern.

Issues

Critical: none.

Important
1. `requests-race.spec.ts:447-462`: the forced-state test does not exercise a race or the overdraw property.
   - Save is 1000 with reserved 1200. `decide_withdrawal` computes `600 > 1000 − 1200 + 600 = 400`, which is true, so it returns `over_available` for each call regardless of ordering. Running it sequentially gives the same result.
   - The result is correct, but it only checks the formula on an invariant-violating state. It passes with or without the lock.
   - Why it matters: the real invariant is `balance(save) >= reserved`, and `decide_withdrawal` never changes `reserved`. Approval is `pending` to `approved`, both in reserved, so parallel approvals cannot overdraw by construction.
   - The test title "never approve beyond Save" overclaims.
   - The sharper test is a different one. Take Save 1000 and create two pending 500s through `create_withdrawal`, which both succeed. Approve both in parallel. Then reduce Save through the admin client, by inserting a Save `out` ledger entry. The reduced balance has to be below the 1000 reserved. Run parallel `complete_request` on both.
   - Both should return `over_available` with 0 ledger rows each and no negative Save. Without the reduction, both complete.
   - Racing a purchase or a `complete_request` against another request is the only real overdraw race, and `post_request` is what guards it.
   - Fix: rename the current test to "decide_withdrawal refuses when reserved exceeds Save (forced state)". Add the create-through-the-function, drop-balance, parallel-complete test. Your suggested Save 1000 with two 500s plus a balance drop is the right shape.
2. `docs/fr-audit.md` FR12 row (unchanged, about line 60): it still says "Goal review and pending requests in the next step (need FR02 and FR14)". FR14 is now Built, so this is stale.    - Overclaim 2: the same row is satisfied only for requests that need approval. An auto-approved or auto-confirmed request notifies nobody. This is by design but is a reading of "a new request". fr-audit.md says it, which is good. Fix: add "(rendering untested in e2e)" or add one inbox assertion to `requests.spec.ts`.

That is a looser reading of the criterion. The e2e at `requests.spec.ts:177` covers over-available, not a repeat request.
2. `docs/testing.md`: the `requests-race` row says "parallel approvals never go beyond Save". Reword it after fixing Important 1.

Spec Compliance
- ✅ Race 1: tests/e2e/requests-race.spec.ts:432-445. Five parallel complete_request give {completed:1, already_completed:4}, status handed_over, 2 ledger rows.
- ✅ Race 3: :485-508. Five parallel create_withdrawal with one key give one distinct request_id and one DB row.
- ✅ Race 4: :510-528. The deposit completes and ends confirmed with 2 ledger rows. The purchase returns not_enough_spend. The outcome is the same whichever call takes the lock first (the hold and the spent balance both leave 0 free). That is correct, but the test cannot show an interleaving.
- ⚠️ Race 2: :447-483. The brief's literal scenario really is unreachable. reserved_save includes pending requests, so create_withdrawal refuses the second 600 against 1000. The two replacements are honest and assert exact statuses and counts.
- ✅ assertParentCanRead (src/features/family/server/dal.ts, around line 119). It requires the parent role and isParentOf(child) and skips only the profile-complete check. It is documented for reads, and assertSelfOrParentOf already uses it. This is the right guard for listRequestsNeedingParent.
- ✅ Mutations stay behind assertParentOf (src/features/requests/server/service.ts:103/116/128: decide, complete, cancel). The DB functions also enforce is_parent_of and raise JA403.
- ✅ The unit test asserts the read uses assertParentCanRead and never calls assertParentOf.

Issues

Critical: none.

Important
1. tests/e2e/requests-race.spec.ts:447-462: the forced-state test does not exercise a race or the overdraw property.
   - Save is 1000 with reserved 1200. decide_withdrawal computes 600 > 1000 − 1200 + 600 = 400, which is true, so it returns over_available for both calls regardless of ordering. Running them sequentially gives the same result.
   - The result is correct, but it only checks the formula on a state that already breaks the invariant. It passes with or without the lock.
   - decide_withdrawal never changes reserved, because pending and approved both count in it. So parallel approvals cannot overdraw by construction.
   - The test title "never approve beyond Save" overclaims.
   - The real overdraw race is a Save drop against complete_request, guarded by post_request.
   - Recommended sharper test, which is your Save 1000 with two 500s plus a balance drop: Save 1000, two pending 500s created through create_withdrawal (both succeed). Approve both in parallel. Then reduce Save through the admin client, by inserting a Save out ledger entry so Save is below the 1000 reserved. Run parallel complete_request on both.
   - Both should return over_available with 0 ledger rows each and no negative Save. Without the drop, both complete.
   - Fix: rename the current test to "decide_withdrawal refuses when reserved exceeds Save (forced state)" and add the balance-drop test.
2. docs/fr-audit.md FR12 row (unchanged, around line 60) still says "pending requests in the next step (need FR02 and FR14)". FR14 is now Built, so this is stale. Fix: reword it. I did not check whether Home now shows pending requests.

2. docs/testing.md `requests-race` row says "parallel approvals never go beyond Save". Reword it after fixing Important 1.

## Final review: PR B (slice 4, savings withdrawals and deposits), 6b07b03..fd6a1ec

### Declined to judge
- Whether the tests pass: I worked read-only and ran nothing (no `pnpm check`, pgTAP or Playwright). The ledger says each task was green.
- RLS, grants and DB-design depth: the parallel security and supabase reviewers cover these. I only read them for correctness and found nothing serious.
- Visual polish against the design tokens: I can't judge that from a diff without a running app.

### Strengths
- The SQL result shapes match the TS unions in `types.ts`, and `repository.ts` parses them strictly. Unknown shapes throw `INTERNAL` instead of being cast through.
- Money posts in exactly one place. `post_request` is the only ledger path, with keys `request:<id>:out|in`. `complete_request` locks the row and then the ledger lock, and `handed_over`/`confirmed` return `already_completed`. The race spec proves 5 parallel completes give 1 `completed` and 2 rows.
- Reservations are consistent. Pending and approved withdrawals reserve Save, pending deposits hold Spend, and purchases share the same ledger lock and see the hold. One window counter, `request_window_total`, is used by caps, auto-approve, auto-confirm and `withdrawal_cap_remaining`.
- Transfers are not shown as income (hard rule 14). History shows one "Save → Spend" / "Spend → Save" line per request with no +/−, and there are tests for it.
- Notice payloads `{amount_minor, reason}` and `{amount_minor, approved}` match the keys `links.ts` reads, and parent request notices link to the child's page, where the items render.
- Every request mutation goes through `runOnline`. Confirm keeps one idempotency key per row across taps, and the take-out sheet makes a fresh key each time it opens. Every new request-time read is inside `<Suspense>`. Parent reads use `assertParentCanRead`, and decide/complete/reject use `assertParentOf`.
- Child copy matches the spec's exact wording. Child Cancel is 44 px, and dates use the family timezone.

### Issues

**Critical:** none.

**Important (fix before merge)**
1. **A parent can cancel without finishing their profile** (`src/features/requests/server/service.ts:137-139`). `cancelRequest` uses `assertSelfOrParentOf`, which for a parent falls back to `assertParentCanRead` (no profile check). So a parent who hasn't finished their profile can cancel a withdrawal through "Didn't hand it over". That breaks ADR 0008 and the rule in `dal.ts`: "every action still goes through assertParentOf". Fix: `const childId = await childOf(id); const user = await assertUser(); if (user.id !== childId) await assertParentOf(childId);`. Update `service.test.ts:84`, which currently locks in the gap.
2. **Wrong inbox copy when a parent taps "Didn't get it"** (`src/features/notifications/links.ts:81-87`). `decide_deposit` always sends `approved: false`, so the child reads "Your parent said not now / You can ask again later." But the child isn't asking for anything here; they handed over cash and the parent says it never arrived. This confuses an 11–14 year old. Fix: give `deposit_decided` with `approved:false` its own copy, e.g. "Your {amount} wasn't added to Save" / "It's still in Spend. Talk to your parent." Optionally link child request notices to `routes.childRequests`.
3. **`docs/testing.md:38` overstates the e2e coverage.** The `requests` row says it covers "caps", but `requests.spec.ts` has no cap scenario. Caps are covered only in pgTAP and unit tests. Either drop "caps" from the row or add a short week-cap e2e step.

**Minor**
4. `docs/database.md:160` says `cancel_request` takes the ledger advisory lock after the row lock. It takes only the row lock. That's harmless, since cancelling only frees money, but fix the doc sentence or add the lock.
5. `docs/fr-audit.md:32`: the "Migration history" row still names `20261030164347_inbox_reminders.sql (this branch)`. It should name `20261030185541_requests.sql` and `20261030190114_request_functions.sql`.
6. `post_request` comments say "re-checks Save only" (`request_functions.sql:218` and the function comment at :269), but the check also keeps every other reservation covered. Make it one line: "Save must cover this and every other reservation." This is also deferred item T14.
7. Child copy says the per-time cap two ways: `describe.ts:23` uses "each time" in the refusal, while the rule list in the same sheet says "per time". Change `PER.item` to "per time" (one line). This is deferred item T11.
8. `rules/describe.ts` `summarise`: when there is both an item rule and a week/month auto rule, the summary mentions only the item one. So it says "Withdrawals over £5 come to you", yet a £3 ask over the weekly total also comes to the parent. This can wait for later polish.
9. Spec §4.7 says the parent's jars show "reserved". The parent pages show the open request items, but no reserved total. Fine to defer to PR E.
10. The "Didn't get it", child-cancel and cap paths have no e2e test. A race test for two different-key `create_withdrawal` calls overdrawing Save is also missing. Both are correct by construction under the ledger lock, so these are test gaps only.

**Triage of deferred ledger items**
- T8: no DB tie of family_id → **leave** (definer functions derive it).
- T8: trim/empty→null → **resolved** (all writers trim).
- T8: no sibling-parent or reserved>balance case → **leave**.
- T9: post_request depends on the JWT → **leave** until a sessionless caller exists.
- T9: explicit deposit branch / raise on unknown kind → **resolved**.
- T9: item over_cap used 0 and already_closed wording → **resolved** in `overCapMessage` and the UI.
- T10: create_deposit ignores post_request's result → **leave**. It can't fail under the same lock with the same arithmetic. Adding `if ... <> 'completed' then raise` is cheap if you want it.
- T10: test gaps (auto-confirm double tap, child cancel releases hold, month window) → **leave** (low risk; covered by shared code paths).
- T10: completed_by = child on auto-confirm → **leave** (documented in database.md).
- T11: over_available returned as ok data → **resolved** (UI checks it).
- T11: transfer treatment in history → **resolved**.
- T11: "each time" vs "per time" → **fix** (Minor 7, one line).
- T11: no test that the reject note reaches the RPC → **leave**.
- T12: refresh after over_available → **leave**.
- T12: CONFLICT text stays on the row after refresh → **leave**.
- T13: substring money matches and the "Close" strict-mode risk → **leave**.
- T14: a second request that still fits is allowed → **leave** (matches the spec).
- T14: the post_request comment → **fix** (Minor 6).

### Verdict
Ready to merge: **With fixes.** Money handling, exactly-once posting and the SQL↔TS↔UI wiring are sound. Fix the profile-gate bypass in `cancelRequest` and the misleading "Didn't get it" inbox copy, and correct the testing.md, database.md and fr-audit doc lines before merging.

timestamp: 2026-10-30T20:05:01.940Z
time: 2026-10-30 20:05
chars: 4777

Security audit of feat/savings-requests (6b07b03..fd6a1ec). Read-only. I did not open any .env* file.

**Critical:** none.

**Important:** none.

**Minor**

1. `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/supabase/migrations/20261030185541_requests.sql:172-182`: `public.held_spend(uuid)` is missing from both the revoke list and the grant list. Postgres gives `PUBLIC` execute on new functions by default, and no migration changes default privileges, so anon can call it. It is not exploitable: the function's own `is_self` / `is_parent_of` check raises JA403 for anon and for other families (pgTAP covers this in `requests.test.sql:77`). But it is the only function in this slice whose grant posture isn't explicit, and it would leak if someone later loosened the body. Fix: add `public.held_spend(uuid)` to both the `revoke ... from public, anon` and the `grant ... to authenticated` lists.

2. `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/supabase/migrations/20261030190114_request_functions.sql:526-529`: `withdrawal_cap_remaining` looks up `family_id` with no `role = 'child' and status = 'approved'` filter, unlike `create_withdrawal` and `create_deposit`. `profile_id` is unique, so only one row can match. The worst case is a cap shown against a non-approved membership's timezone window. It affects display only, because enforcement happens in `create_withdrawal`. Fix: use the same filtered lookup as `create_withdrawal`, for consistency.

**What I checked and found correct**

- **RLS on `requests`:** RLS is on. Anon has no access, and authenticated users cannot insert, update, delete or truncate. The only policy is SELECT for `is_self(child_id) or is_parent_of(child_id)`. `is_parent_of` requires an approved parent and an approved child in the same family, so another family's parent cannot read anything.
- **Who can act:**
  - `decide_withdrawal`, `decide_deposit` and `complete_request` all lock the row, then require `is_parent_of`. A child gets JA403.
  - A missing request and another family's request both return JA403, so existence doesn't leak. The service layer does the same: `childOf` throws FORBIDDEN, then `assertParentOf`.
  - `cancel_request` limits a child to their own pending request. Parents are refused on deposits.
- **Internal functions have no grants:** `post_request` (request_functions.sql:560), `request_window_total` (requests.sql:169), `rule_window_start` (from the earlier slice), and `notify_parents` / `notify_child`.
- **Definer functions:** every new one has `set search_path = ''` and uses schema-qualified names (`public.*`, `pg_catalog.*`).
- **Auto-approve and auto-confirm:** they come only from `money_rules` rows, which children cannot write (writes are revoked from authenticated and there is a SELECT-only policy). They are evaluated under the child's ledger advisory lock.
- **No caller-supplied time:** windows use `pg_catalog.now()`, `occurred_at` defaults to `now()`, and no function takes a time argument.
- **Idempotency keys:** lookups are scoped to `child_id = p_child_id`, and that id must pass `is_self`. A key cannot reach another child's request, and the JA409 kind-mismatch error only reveals the caller's own data. `complete_request`'s key is not stored, and ledger keys derive from the request id, so posting happens exactly once.
- **Notice payloads:** amounts, the approved flag, and the child's own reason (sent to their parents only). No decision note and no PII.
- **Error handling:**
  - `toAppError` maps database errors to safe messages and logs only code and message.
  - `unexpected()` puts the raw RPC result (statuses, ids, amounts) only in the cause.
  - `CONFLICT` uses fixed copy.
- **Zod:** every action goes through `createAction` with a schema. Ids and keys are `z.uuid()`, amounts use `moneySchema`, text is trimmed and capped at 140 (the database enforces the same), and `approve` is a strict boolean or "true"/"false".
- **Server-side identity:** `createWithdrawal` and `createDeposit` take `childId` from `assertRole("child")`, not from input. Parent reads use `assertParentCanRead`; parent actions use `assertParentOf`.
- **Client code:** no admin client in `src/features/requests`, no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, and no `process.env` reads.
- **Public repo:** the diff and the new `.claude-logs/2026-10-30_21-12-45_14c1a4e8-...md` contain no credential-shaped strings. The only emails are `example.com` test addresses and a GitHub noreply address. E2E passwords are random UUIDs and the secret key comes from the local Supabase loader (same as existing specs). `scripts/check-secrets.sh` exits 0. The change adds no new credential type, so the redaction and gitleaks coverage doesn't need updating.

**Verdict:** OK to merge. Nothing blocks it; adding the `held_spend` grants (Minor 1) is worth doing.

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=SUBAGENT_RESPONSE num=1 session=14c1a4e8-25b8-4869-859a-f799b43c74df agent=a6fbfcdbbfc00e5e8]
timestamp: 2026-10-30T20:06:45.729Z
time: 2026-10-30 20:06
chars: 7269

Review: savings requests migrations on feat/savings-requests vs main 6b07b03. I reviewed the code statically. I could not run `supabase status`, `db reset` or `db test` because the Bash permission was denied, so none of the pgTAP or e2e results below were executed by me.

## Critical
None.

## Important

1. **No concurrency test for the main reservation race.** `tests/e2e/requests-race.spec.ts` covers one key sent five times (line ~283), concurrent `complete_request`, concurrent approvals and complete vs purchase. It never sends several `create_withdrawal` calls with *different* keys at once. That is the path where `reserved_save` and the week cap could be oversold if the ledger lock were missing or taken too late.
   - Fix: add a test with Save 1000 and five parallel 300 asks with distinct keys. Expect `{pending: 3, over_available: 2}` and `reserved_save = 900`.
   - Add a cap variant: week cap 800, parallel 300s, expect two pending and the rest `over_cap`.
   - Optionally add a parallel `create_deposit` vs `record_purchase` race. The current one races `complete_request` against the purchase, not create.

## Minor

1. **The "conservative" re-check is really a whole-account check, and pending asks can block approved hand-overs.**
   - `20261030190114_request_functions.sql:174-175` (`amount > balance - reserved + amount`) and `:220-221` / `:242-243` (`amount > balance - (reserved - amount)`) both reduce to `reserved_save > balance(save)` (or `held_spend > balance(spend)`). The request's own amount cancels out.
   - So once Save is over-reserved, nothing can be approved or handed over until something is cancelled or rejected. Even a *pending*, undecided ask blocks handing over an *approved* one. Example: Save drops 1000 → 900 with approved 600 and pending 400; the 600 hand-over is refused.
   - Today this state can only be reached out-of-band. No function moves money out of Save except `post_request`, and every Spend outflow keeps balance ≥ held. So it is safe, and the race spec documents it on purpose (line ~255).
   - Fix for when Save reversals or corrections arrive: decide the policy explicitly. For example, post_request only protects *other approved* reservations (`balance - sum(other approved) >= amount`). In any case, write the condition as `reserved_save(child) > balance(...)` so the meaning is obvious.

2. **`cancel_request` takes no ledger lock, but the docs say it does.** Code is `20261030190114_request_functions.sql:469-498`; `docs/database.md:160` says it takes "the ledger advisory lock". Not taking it is safe: cancelling only frees money, and the row lock already serialises it against decide and complete. Fix: correct the doc, or add the lock for uniformity.

3. **`cancel_request` does not record who cancelled or when** (`:494`). A child cancel and a parent cancel of an approved withdrawal look the same. Fix: set `decided_by = auth.uid(), decided_at = now()` (or completed_*) on cancel.

4. **Auto-approved withdrawal leaves `decided_at` null** (`:116-123`). Fix: set `decided_at = now()` when `v_status = 'approved'`, so `approved` always implies `decided_at is not null`. Optionally add that as a check constraint.

5. **`create_deposit` ignores `post_request`'s return value** (`:404`). If the "cannot be refused" assumption ever breaks, the row stays `pending` but the function sets `auto = true` and returns `confirmed`. Fix: `if public.post_request(v_id, p_child_id) <> 'completed' then raise ...`. Also put `auto` in the insert instead of doing a second update (`:405`).

6. **`post_request` does not guard itself** (`:212`). It trusts the caller for the status check and the lock. It has no grants, so this is fine today. For defence in depth: `select ... for update` and raise unless `(withdrawal, approved)` or `(deposit, pending)`.

7. **Indexes** (`20261030185541_requests.sql:32`).
   - `(child_id, status)` serves the open-items query (repository.ts:113-120) and `reserved_save` / `held_spend`.
   - The history list (repository.ts:101-105, `order by occurred_at desc limit`) and `request_window_total` (`occurred_at >= start`) filter or sort by `occurred_at`, which the index does not cover.
   - Fine at ~60 users. If wanted, add `(child_id, kind, occurred_at desc)` for both.

8. **`withdrawal_cap_remaining` looks up the family without the `role = 'child' and status = 'approved'` filter** (`:526-529`). This is harmless because `family_members.profile_id` is unique, but it is inconsistent with the create functions.

9. **pgTAP gaps** (all cheap to add):
   - a parent calling `cancel_request` on a deposit → `22023`;
   - `decide_withdrawal` on a deposit → `22023`;
   - missing request id → `JA403` for decide_withdrawal / complete / cancel;
   - `JA409` for `create_withdrawal` reusing a deposit key (only the reverse is tested);
   - a month cap;
   - cap and auto-approve together (cap refuses first);
   - anon on `create_withdrawal`.

## Verified OK
- **Lock order:** decide, complete and decide_deposit take the row `for update`, then `ledger:<child>`. create_* take the advisory lock only, then insert a new row. No function takes the advisory lock and then waits on an existing request row. This matches `record_purchase` (advisory only) and handover (row, then advisory), so no deadlock cycle.
- **Retries after the lock:** the `(child, key)` retry lookup runs after the lock, so a retry never hits a unique-violation race.
- **Exactly-once posting:** status is checked under the row lock (a waiter re-reads the updated row), and ledger keys `request:<id>:out|in` are globally unique and don't clash with the `purchase:`, `occurrence:`, `parent_gift:` or `reversal:` keys. All ledger kinds and `source_type 'request'` exist in the ledger checks. All notification kinds and `source_type 'request'` exist in the notifications checks.
- **Hold arithmetic:** `record_purchase` keeps Spend ≥ held both with overspend on and off. A deposit's own hold is released correctly in the re-check, and the auto-confirm path is consistent with it.
- **RLS:** enabled, with a select-only policy (`is_self or is_parent_of`). Inserts, updates, deletes and truncate are revoked from authenticated, and everything is revoked from anon. A sibling or another family sees 0 rows and gets `JA403` (tested).
- **Grants:** every definer function has `set search_path = ''`. `post_request` and `request_window_total` are revoked from public, anon and authenticated (42501 tested). `create or replace held_spend` keeps the signature, return type and its existing grant from `20261029183422_purchases.sql:365-375` (the child calling `held_spend` in requests.test.sql proves it).
- **Migration hygiene:** two additive migrations with timestamps after main's last one (`20261030164347`). No drops or alters of existing objects. All dependencies exist (`set_updated_at`, `notify_*`, `rule_window_start`, `is_*`). Safe for the GitHub integration.
- **Types:** `src/types/database.types.ts` was regenerated and includes the requests table and functions.

**Verdict:** No blockers and the migrations look safe to apply. Before merge, add the parallel `create_withdrawal` race test with distinct keys and fix the `cancel_request` lock doc, and decide the re-check policy before any Save-reversal feature ships.

1. ADDRESSED. src/features/requests/server/service.ts:136-141 resolves childOf, then assertUser. A caller whose id is the child is allowed. 2. ADDRESSED. links.ts:83-88 applies only to kind === "deposit_decided" with approved === false. Title is "Your {amount} wasn't added to Save", with a fallback title when there is no amount. Detail is "It's still in Spend. Talk to your parent." The copy has no exclamation marks or shame words. The withdrawal declined branch and the approved branch are untouched, and links.test.ts asserts the withdrawal copy exactly.
3. ADDRESSED. tests/e2e/requests-race.spec.ts adds children ReqG and ReqH (indices 6 and 7, matching beforeAll). Each ask uses its own randomUUID key.
   - Save 1000, five 300 asks: expects {pending:3, over_available:2} and reserved_save 900. The count is deterministic because the ledger lock serialises the calls.
   - Week cap 800, five 300 asks: expects {pending:2, over_cap:3} and reserved 600. The third ask reaches 900, above the cap, and Save 1000 never triggers over_available.
   - Cleanup is scoped to the spec's own family (delete families row, then the auth users).
4. ADDRESSED. tests/e2e/requests.spec.ts:~185-194 adds a week cap 400 step. The expected message "US$700 already" is consistent with the 500 handed over plus 200 approved earlier. docs/testing.md reworded the row to "a week cap", so the claim now matches. The implementer reports this passing. I did not run it.
5. ADDRESSED. 20261030185541_requests.sql adds held_spend to both the revoke list (from public, anon) and the grant list (to authenticated).
6. ADDRESSED. request_functions.sql:~532-536 filters with role = 'child' and status = 'approved'.
7. ADDRESSED. docs/database.md corrects both the cancel_request line and the lock-order paragraph.
8. ADDRESSED. request_functions.sql:~118-126 sets decided_at = now() when the status is approved, and decided_by stays null. The check constraints (requests_withdrawal_never_confirmed, requests_deposit_never_approved) do not involve decided_at. The TS mapping does not read decided_at. The new pgTAP assertion is in withdrawals.test.sql (plan 67).
9. ADDRESSED. create_deposit sets auto = v_has_auto and v_auto_ok on insert. That expression can never be null, because both booleans are initialised non-null. The second update is gone. It now raises 22023 if post_request returns anything but 'completed'.
10. ADDRESSED. The wording is updated in the inline comment at ~:217 and in the function comment, for both the Save and Spend cases.
11. ADDRESSED. describe.ts PER.item is now "per time", and describe.test.ts is updated.
12. ADDRESSED. docs/fr-audit.md names both migrations.

Named risks
- cancelRequest: no new hole. A sibling child fails user.id !== childId and then fails assertRole("parent"). The database also enforces is_self / is_parent_of with JA403, so there are two layers.
- create_deposit raising 22023: the raise is inside the function, so the whole transaction rolls back, including the inserted request and any ledger rows. The branch is unreachable in practice, because Spend is checked under the same lock just before. If it ever fired, 22023 would map to VALIDATION ("Please check what you entered", src/lib/db-error.ts:15), which is slightly misleading for an internal fault. I count that as acceptable and Minor.

New breakage: none found.

Minor (deferred)
- A 22023 raise for an impossible post_request result would be more accurate as an internal error code (for example XX000 or P0001). That would map to an internal error rather than VALIDATION.
- The overCapMessage in the new requests.spec step reports used US$700 against a cap of 400, which is correct but reads oddly. This is not a bug.
- The cancel_request function comment at request_functions.sql:508 still says "Locks the row", which is accurate.

Verdict: all findings addressed
